\omega\in L^{2}(\Lambda^{k}T^{*}\!M)
for which there exists
\rho\in L^{2}(\Lambda^{k-1}T^{*}\!M)
such that for every
\eta\in{\rm Test}(\Lambda^{k-1}T^{*}\!M)
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{M}\langle\rho,\eta\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{m}=\int_{M}\langle\omega,\mathrm{d}\eta\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{m}.

If it exists,
\rho
is unique, denoted by
\mathrm{d}_{*}\omega
and called the
codifferential
of
\omega
. We simply define

D(\mathrm{d}_{*}^{0}):=L^{0}(M;\mathfrak{m})
and
\mathrm{d}_{*}:=0
on this space.

By the density of
{\rm Test}(\Lambda^{k-1}T^{*}\!M)
in
L^{2}(\Lambda^{k-1}T^{*}\!M)
, the uniqueness statement is indeed true.
Furthermore,
\mathrm{d}_{*}
is a closed operator, i.e., the image of the assignment
{\rm Id}\times\mathrm{d}_{*}:D(\mathrm{d}_{*}^{k})\to L^{2}(\Lambda^{k}T^{*}\!M)\times L^{2}(\Lambda^{k-1}T^{*}\!M)
is closed in
L^{2}(\Lambda^{k}T^{*}\!M)\times L^{2}(\Lambda^{k-1}T^{*}\!M)
.

By way of
[
12
, Theorem 7.5 and Lemma 7.15]
, we have the following:
For
f\in D(\mathscr{E})_{e}\cap L^{\infty}(M;\mathfrak{m})
and
\omega\in H^{1,2}(T^{*}\!M)
satisfying
\mathrm{d}f\in L^{\infty}(M;\mathfrak{m})
or
\omega\in L^{\infty}(T^{*}\!M)
,

f\omega\in H^{1,2}(T^{*}\!M)
with

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}(f\omega)

\displaystyle=f\mathrm{d}\omega+\mathrm{d}f\land\omega,

(6.1)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{*}(f\omega)

\displaystyle=f\mathrm{d}_{*}\omega-\langle\mathrm{d}f,\omega\rangle.

(6.2)

6.2

The Sobolev spaces
W^{1,p}(\Lambda^{k}T^{*}\!M)
and
H^{1,p}(\Lambda^{k+1}T^{*}\!M)

Definition 6.4

(The space
W^{1,2}(\Lambda^{k}T^{*}\!M)
)
.

We define the space
W^{1,2}(\Lambda^{k}T^{*}\!M)
by

\displaystyle W^{1,2}(\Lambda^{k}T^{*}\!M):=D(\mathrm{d}^{k})\cap D(\mathrm{d}_{*}^{k}).

By
[
12
, Theorem 7.5 and Lemma 7.15]
, we already know that
W^{1,2}(\Lambda^{k}T^{*}\!M)
is a dense subspace of

L^{2}(\Lambda^{k}T^{*}\!M)
.

We endow
W^{1,2}(\Lambda^{k}T^{*}\!M)
with the norm
\|\cdot\|_{W^{1,2}(\Lambda^{k}T^{*}\!M)}
given by

\displaystyle\|\omega\|_{W^{1,2}(\Lambda^{k}T^{*}\!M)}^{2}:=\|\omega\|_{L^{2}(\Lambda^{k}T^{*}\!M)}^{2}+\|\mathrm{d}\omega\|_{L^{2}(\Lambda^{k+1}T^{*}\!M)}^{2}+\|\mathrm{d}_{*}\omega\|_{L^{2}(\Lambda^{k-1}T^{*}\!M)}^{2}

and we define the
contravariant
functional
\mathscr{E}_{\rm con}:L^{2}(\Lambda^{k}T^{*}\!M)\to[0,+\infty]
by

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{\rm con}(\omega):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\displaystyle{\int_{M}\left[|\mathrm{d}\omega|^{2}+|\mathrm{d}_{*}\omega|^{2}\right]\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{m}}&\text{ if }\omega\in W^{1,2}(\Lambda^{k}T^{*}\!M),\\
\infty&\text{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

Arguing as for
[
12
, Theorems 5.3, 6.3 and 7.5]
,
W^{1,2}(\Lambda^{k}T^{*}\!M)
becomes a separable Hilbert space with respect to
\|\cdot\|_{W^{1,2}(\Lambda^{k}T^{*}\!M)}
. moreover, the functional
\mathscr{E}_{\rm con}:L^{2}(\Lambda^{k}T^{*}\!M)\to[0,+\infty]
is clearly lower semi continuous.

Again by
[
12
, Theorem 7.5 and Lemma 7.15]
, we have
{\rm Reg}(\Lambda^{k}T^{*}\!M)\subset W^{1,2}(\Lambda^{k}T^{*}\!M)
, so that the following definition makes sense.

Definition 6.5

(The space
H^{1,2}(\Lambda^{k}T^{*}\!M)
)
.

The space
H^{1,2}(\Lambda^{k}T^{*}\!M)\subset W^{1,2}(\Lambda^{k}T^{*}\!M)
is defined by
the closure of
{\rm Reg}(\Lambda^{k}T^{*}\!M)
with respect to
\|\cdot\|_{W^{1,2}(\Lambda^{k}T^{*}\!M)}
:

\displaystyle H^{1,2}(\Lambda^{k}T^{*}\!M):=\overline{{\rm Reg}(\Lambda^{k}T^{*}\!M)}^{\|\cdot\|_{W^{1,2}(\Lambda^{k}T^{*}\!M)}}.

6.3

Hodge-Kodaira Laplacian

We now define the Hosdge-Kodaira Laplacian in L
2
-sense:

Definition 6.6

(
L^{2}
-Hodge-Kodaira Laplacian
{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK}_{k}
)
.

The space
D({\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK}_{k})
is defined to consist of all
\omega\in H^{1,2}(\Lambda^{k}T^{*}\!M)
for which there exists
\alpha\in L^{2}(\Lambda^{k}T^{*}\!M)
such that for every
\eta\in H^{1,2}(\Lambda^{k}T^{*}\!M)
,

\displaystyle\int_{M}\langle\alpha,\eta\rangle\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{m}=-\int_{M}\left[\langle\mathrm{d}\omega,\mathrm{d}\eta\rangle+\langle\mathrm{d}_{*}\omega,\mathrm{d}_{*}\eta\rangle\right]\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{m}.

In case of existence, the element
\alpha
is unique, denoted by
{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK}_{k}\omega
and called the

Hodge Laplacian
,
Hodge-Kodaira Laplacian
or
Hodge-de ​​Rham Laplacian
of
\omega
.
Formally
{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK}_{k}\omega
can be written “
{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK}_{k}\omega=-(\mathrm{d}\mathrm{d}_{*}+\mathrm{d}_{*}\mathrm{d})\omega
”.

For the most important case
k=1
, we write
{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK}
instead of
{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK}_{1}
. We see
{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK}_{0}=\Delta
the usual
L^{2}
-generator associated to the given quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet form
(\mathscr{E},D(\mathscr{E}))
. Moreover, the Hodge-Kodaira Laplacian
{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK}_{k}
is a closed operator.

6.4

Heat flow of
1
-forms associated with
{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK}

We define the functional
\widetilde{\mathscr{E}}_{\rm con}:L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)\to[0,+\infty]
with

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathscr{E}}_{\rm con}(\omega):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\displaystyle{\int_{M}\left[|\mathrm{d}\omega|^{2}+|\mathrm{d}_{*}\omega|^{2}\right]\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{m}}&\text{ if }\omega\in H^{1,2}(T^{*}\!M),\\
\infty&\text{ otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

We write
\mathscr{E}\hskip-8.5359pt\mathscr{E}^{\rm HK}
instead of
\widetilde{\mathscr{E}}_{\rm con}
. Let
(P_{t}^{\rm HK})_{t\geq 0}
be the heat semigroup of bounded linear and self-adjoint operator on
L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)
formally written by

\displaystyle\text{``$P_{t}^{\rm HK}:=e^{t\,{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK}}$''}.

The following are important:

Lemma 6.7

(
[
12
, Lemma 8.32, Corollaries 8.35 and 8.36]
)
.

We have the following:

(1)

For every
f\in D(\mathscr{E})
and every
t>0
,
\mathrm{d}P_{t}f\in D({\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK})
and

\displaystyle P_{t}^{\rm HK}\mathrm{d}f=\mathrm{d}P_{t}f.

(6.3)

(2)

If
\omega\in D(\mathrm{d}_{*})
and
t>0
, then
P_{t}^{\rm HK}\omega\in D(\mathrm{d}_{*})
and

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{*}P_{t}^{\rm HK}\omega=P_{t}\mathrm{d}_{*}\omega.

(6.4)

(3)

\inf\sigma(-\Delta^{\kappa})\leq\inf\sigma(-{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK})
.

The formulas (
6.3
) and (
6.4
) are called
intertwining properties
, which play
a crucial role to prove the
L^{p}
-boundedness of Riesz operator.

The following theorem is known as
Hess-Schrader-Uhlenbrock inequality
.
This is proved in
[
12
, Theorem 8.41]
under an extra assumption
[
12
, Assumption 8.37]
in the framework of tamed Dirichlet space (Assumption
1
). In
[
39
]
, we improve

[
12
, Theorem 8.41]
without this assumption.
Theorem
6.8
also plays a crucial role to prove the
L^{p}
-boundedness of Riesz operator.

Theorem 6.8

(Hess-Schrader-Uhlenbrock inequality,
[
39
, Theorem 1.2]
)
.

We have the following:

(1)

For every
\omega\in L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)
and
\alpha>C_{\kappa}
,

\displaystyle|R_{\alpha}^{\rm HK}\omega|\leq R_{\alpha}^{\kappa}|\omega|\quad\mathfrak{m}\text{-a.e.}

(6.5)

(2)

For every
\omega\in L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)
and every
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle|P_{t}^{\rm HK}\omega|\leq P_{t}^{\kappa}|\omega|\quad\mathfrak{m}\text{-a.e.}

(6.6)

Theorem
6.8
(ii) is proved in

[
11
, Theorem A]
in the framework of RCD
(K,\infty)
-space for
K\in\mathbb{R}
, and also
proved in

[
12
, Theorem 8.41]
in the present framework with an extra assumption
[
12
, Assumption 8.37]
.

Corollary 6.9

(
C_{0}
-property of
(P_{t}^{\rm HK})_{t\geq 0}
on
L^{p}(M;\mathfrak{m})
,
[
39
, Corollary 1.3]
)
.

Suppose
p\in[2,+\infty]
, or
\kappa^{-}\in S_{K}({\bf X})
and
p\in[1,+\infty]
.
Then the heat flow
(P_{t}^{\rm HK})_{t\geq 0}
can be extended to a semigroup on
L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)
and
for each
t>0

\displaystyle\|P_{t}^{\rm HK}\omega\|_{L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)}\leq C(\kappa)e^{C_{\kappa}t}\|\omega\|_{L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)},\quad\omega\in L^{p}(T^{*}\!M).

(6.7)

Moreover, if
\kappa^{-}\in S_{K}({\bf X})
and
p\in[1,+\infty[
, then

(P_{t}^{\rm HK})_{t\geq 0}
is strongly continuous on
L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)
, i.e.,

(P_{t}^{\rm HK})_{t\geq 0}
is a
C_{0}
-semigroup on
L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)
, and further

(P_{t}^{\rm HK})_{t\geq 0}
is weakly* continuous on
L^{\infty}(T^{*}\!M)
.

7

Ricci curvature measures

To state the notion of Ricci curvature measure, we need preliminary preparation.

The following are shown in
[
12
, Lemmas 8.1 and 8.2]
:

Lemma 7.1

(
[
12
, Lemma 8.1]
)
.

For every
g\in{\rm Test}(M)\cup\mathbb{R}{\bf 1}_{M}
and every
f\in{\rm Test}(M)
. we have
g\,\mathrm{d}f\in D({\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK})
with

\displaystyle{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK}(g\,\mathrm{d}f)=g\,\mathrm{d}\Delta f+\Delta g\,\mathrm{d}f+2{\rm Hess}\,f(\nabla g,\cdot),

with the usual interpretations
\nabla{\bf 1}_{M}=0
and
\Delta{\bf 1}_{M}=0
. More generally, for every
X\in{\rm Reg}(TM)
and every
h\in{\rm Test}(M)\cup\mathbb{R}{\bf 1}_{M}
, we have
hX^{\flat}\in D({\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK})
with

\displaystyle{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK}(hX^{\flat})=h{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK}X^{\flat}-{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK}hX^{\flat}+2(\nabla_{\nabla h}X)^{\flat}.

Lemma 7.2

(
[
12
, Lemma 8.2]
)
.

For every
X\in{\rm Reg}(TM)
, we have
|X|^{2}\in D({\text{\boldmath$\Delta$}}^{2\kappa})
with

\displaystyle{\text{\boldmath$\Delta$}}^{2\kappa}\frac{|X|^{2}}{2}\geq\left[|\nabla X|_{\rm HS}^{2}+\langle X,({\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK}X^{\flat})^{\sharp}\rangle\right]\mathfrak{m}.

Definition 7.3

(The space
H^{1,2}_{\sharp}(TM)
)
.

We define the space
H^{1,2}_{\sharp}(TM)
as the image of
H^{1,2}(T^{*}\!M)
under the map
\sharp
, endowed with the norm

\displaystyle\|X\|_{H^{1,2}_{\sharp}(TM)}:=\|X^{\flat}\|_{H^{1,2}(T^{*}\!M)}.

The following is proved in
[
12
, Lemma 8.8]
:

Lemma 7.4

(
[
12
, Lemma 8.8]
)
.

H^{1,2}_{\sharp}(TM)
is a subspace of
H^{1,2}(TM)
. The aforementioned natural inclusion is continuous. Additionally, for every
X\in H^{1,2}_{\sharp}(TM)
,

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{\rm cov}(X)\leq\mathscr{E}_{\rm con}(X^{\flat})-\langle\kappa,|X|^{2}\rangle.

Then, we can state the main result of
[
12
]
:

Theorem 7.5

(
[
12
, Theorem 8.9]
)
.

There exists a unique continuous mapping
{\bf Ric}^{\kappa}:H^{1,2}_{\sharp}(TM)^{2}\to\mathfrak{M}_{\rm f}^{\pm}(M)_{\mathscr{E}}
satisfying the identity

\displaystyle{\bf Ric}^{\kappa}(X,Y)={\text{\boldmath$\Delta$}}^{2\kappa}\frac{\langle X,Y\rangle}{2}-\left[\frac{1}{2}\langle X,({\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK}Y^{\flat})^{\sharp}\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\langle Y,({\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK}X^{\flat})^{\sharp}\rangle+\langle\nabla X\,|\,\nabla Y\rangle\right]\mathfrak{m}

(7.1)

for every
X,Y\in{\rm Reg}(TM)
. Here
\mathfrak{M}_{\rm f}^{\pm}(M)_{\mathscr{E}}
denotes the family of finite signed smooth measures.
The map
{\bf Ric}^{\kappa}
is symmetric and
\mathbb{R}
-linear. Furthermore, for every
X,Y\in H^{1,2}_{\sharp}(TM)
, it obeys

\displaystyle{\bf Ric}^{\kappa}(X,X)

\displaystyle\geq 0,

(7.2)

\displaystyle{\bf Ric}^{\kappa}(X,Y)(M)

\displaystyle=\int_{M}\left[\langle\mathrm{d}X^{\flat},\mathrm{d}Y^{\flat}\rangle+\langle\mathrm{d}_{*}X^{\flat},\mathrm{d}_{*}Y^{\flat}\rangle\right]\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{m}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt-\int_{M}\langle\nabla X\,|\,\nabla Y\rangle\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{m}-\langle\kappa,\widetilde{\langle X,Y\rangle}\rangle,

(7.3)

\displaystyle\|{\bf Ric}^{\kappa}(X,Y)\|_{\rm TV}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left[\mathscr{E}_{\rm con}(X^{\flat})-\langle\kappa,|X|^{2}\rangle\right]\left[\mathscr{E}_{\rm con}(Y^{\flat})-\langle\kappa,|Y|^{2}\rangle\right].

(7.4)

Keeping in mind Theorem
7.5
, we define the map

{\bf Ric}:H^{1,2}_{\sharp}(TM)^{2}\to\mathfrak{M}_{\rm f}^{\pm}(M)_{\mathscr{E}}
by

\displaystyle{\bf Ric}(X,Y):={\bf Ric}^{\kappa}(X,Y)+\langle\kappa,\widetilde{\langle X,Y\rangle}\rangle.

(7.5)

This map is well-defined, symmetric and
\mathbb{R}
-bilinear.
{\bf Ric}
is also jointly continuous, which follows from polarization,
[
12
, Corollary 6.14]
and Lemma
7.4
. Lastly, by Theorem
7.5
, Lemma
7.2
and
[
12
, Lemma 8.12]
, for every
X,Y\in{\rm Reg}(TM)
, we have a similar expression
with (
7.1
):

\displaystyle{\bf Ric}(X,Y)={\text{\boldmath$\Delta$}}\frac{\langle X,Y\rangle}{2}-\left[\frac{1}{2}\langle X,({\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK}Y^{\flat})^{\sharp}\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\langle Y,({\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK}X^{\flat})^{\sharp}\rangle+\langle\nabla X\,|\,\nabla Y\rangle\right]\mathfrak{m}

(7.6)

Denote by
{\bf Ric}_{\ll}^{\kappa}(X,Y)
(resp.
{\bf Ric}_{\perp}^{\kappa}(X,Y)
) the absolutely continuous (resp. singular) part of
{\bf Ric}^{\kappa}(X,Y)
for
X,Y\in H^{1,2}_{\sharp}(TM)
with respect to
\mathfrak{m}
. Then we see
{\bf Ric}_{\ll}^{\kappa}(X,X)\geq 0
and

{\bf Ric}_{\perp}^{\kappa}(X,X)\geq 0
for
X\in H^{1,2}_{\sharp}(TM)
, which yields

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{rl}{\bf Ric}_{\ll}(X,X)&={\bf Ric}_{\ll}^{\kappa}(X,X)+\widetilde{|X|^{2}}\kappa_{\ll}\geq\widetilde{|X|^{2}}\kappa_{\ll},\\
{\bf Ric}_{\perp}(X,X)&={\bf Ric}_{\perp}^{\kappa}(X,X)+\widetilde{|X|^{2}}\kappa_{\perp}\geq\widetilde{|X|^{2}}\kappa_{\perp}\end{array}\right.

for
X\in H^{1,2}_{\sharp}(TM)
.
Here
\kappa_{\ll}
(resp.
\kappa_{\perp}
) is the absolutely continuous (resp. singular) part of
\kappa
with respect to
\mathfrak{m}
.
We define the Ricci tensor
\mathfrak{Ric}(X,Y)
by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Ric}(X,Y):=\frac{\mathrm{d}{\bf Ric}_{\ll}(X,Y)}{\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{m}}\quad\text{ for }\quad X,Y\in H^{1,2}_{\sharp}(TM).

(7.9)

In particular, by (
7
)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Ric}(X,X)\geq|X|^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}\kappa_{\ll}}{\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{m}}\quad\text{ for }\quad X\in H^{1,2}_{\sharp}(TM).

(7.10)

8

Littlewood-Paley
G
-functions

Recall the heat flow
(P_{t}^{\rm HK})_{t\geq 0}
of Hodge-Kodaira Laplacian
{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK}
acting on
L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)
.
By Corollary
6.9
,
(P_{t}^{\rm HK})_{t\geq 0}
can be
regarded as a bounded semigroup on
L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)
.
Let
(Q_{t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}})_{t\geq 0}
be the
\alpha
-order Cauchy semigroup acting on
L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)
defined by

\displaystyle Q_{t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\theta:=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\alpha s}P_{s}^{\rm HK}\theta\lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}s),\quad\theta\in L^{p}(T^{*}\!M),

where
\lambda_{t}
with
t\geq 0
is the probability measure on
[0,+\infty[
, whose Laplace transform is given by

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\gamma s}\lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}s)=e^{-\sqrt{\gamma}t},\quad\gamma\geq 0.

For
t>0
,
\lambda_{t}
has the following expression

\displaystyle\lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}s)=\frac{t}{2\sqrt{\pi}}e^{-t^{2}/4s}s^{-3/2}\mathrm{d}s.

For
\theta\in L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)
,
Q_{t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\theta\in D({\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK})
and

\displaystyle\|{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK}Q_{t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\theta\|_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)}\leq\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\alpha s}\frac{1}{s}\lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}s)\right)\|\theta\|_{L^{2}T^{*}\!M)}<\infty.

Q_{t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\theta
can be formally written by

\displaystyle Q_{t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\theta(x)=\text{``$e^{-\sqrt{\alpha-{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK}}\,t}\theta(x)$''.}

By Corollary
6.9
and
\kappa^{-}\in S_{K}({\bf X})
, we see that

\displaystyle\|Q_{t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\theta\|_{L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)}\leq Ce^{-\sqrt{\alpha-C_{\kappa}}t}\|\theta\|_{L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)}

(8.1)

holds for any
p\in[1,+\infty]

and
(Q_{t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}})_{t\geq 0}
is a strongly continuous semigroup on
L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)
for
p\in[1,+\infty[
and
\alpha\geq C_{\kappa}
and
weakly* continuous on
L^{\infty}(T^{*}\!M)
in the same way of the proof of

[
39
, Corollary 1.3]
.
The infinitesimal generator of
(Q_{t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}})_{t\geq 0}
on
L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)
is denoted by

-\sqrt{\alpha-{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK}_{p}}
, where
{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK}_{p}
is the infinitesimal generator of
(P_{t}^{\rm HK})_{t\geq 0}
on
L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)
.
We may omit the subscript
p
for simplicity.

For
\theta\in L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)\cap L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)
and
\alpha\geq C_{\kappa}
, we define the Littlewood-Paley’s
G
-functions by

\displaystyle{g_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rightarrow}}{{\theta}}}}(x,t)

\displaystyle:=\left|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}(Q_{t}^{(\alpha),\rm HK}\theta)(x)\right|,\qquad\qquad\quad{G_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rightarrow}}{{\theta}}}}(x):=\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}t{g_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rightarrow}}{{\theta}}}}(x,t)^{2}\mathrm{d}t\right)^{1/2},

\displaystyle{g_{\theta}^{\uparrow}}(x,t)

\displaystyle:=\left|\nabla(Q_{t}^{(\alpha),\rm HK}\theta)^{\sharp}(x)\right|_{\rm HS},\qquad\quad\quad{G_{\theta}^{\uparrow}}(x):=\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}t{g_{\theta}^{\uparrow}}(x,t)^{2}\mathrm{d}t\right)^{1/2},

\displaystyle g_{\theta}(x,t)

\displaystyle:=\sqrt{{g_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rightarrow}}{{\theta}}}}(x,t)^{2}+{g_{\theta}^{\uparrow}}(x,t)^{2}},\qquad\quad G_{\theta}(x):=\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}tg_{\theta}(x,t)^{2}\mathrm{d}t\right)^{1/2}.

Lemma 8.1

(Maximal ergodic inequality,
[
50
, Theorem 3.3]
)
.

Suppose
p\in]1,+\infty[
. For
f\in L^{p}(M;\mathfrak{m})
,

\displaystyle\left\|\sup_{t\geq 0}P_{t}|f|\right\|_{L^{p}(M;\mathfrak{m})}\leq\frac{p}{p-1}\|f\|_{L^{p}(M;m)}.

(8.2)

Proposition 8.2
.

Suppose that
\kappa^{-}\in S_{K}({\bf X})
and
p\in]1,+\infty[
. Then there exists
C_{p}>0
such that for any
\alpha\geq C_{\kappa}
,

\displaystyle\int_{M}\sup_{t\geq 0}\left|Q_{t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\theta(x)\right|^{p}\mathfrak{m}(\mathrm{d}x)\leq C_{p}\|\theta\|_{L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)}^{p},\quad\theta\in L^{p}(T^{*}\!M).

Proof.

By Theorem
6.8
,

\displaystyle\left|Q_{t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\theta(x)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\alpha s}P_{s}^{\rm HK}\theta(x)\lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}s)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\alpha s}\left|P_{s}^{\rm HK}\theta(x)\right|\lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}s)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\alpha s}P_{s}^{\kappa}|\theta|(x)\lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}s)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}_{x}\left[e^{-\alpha s-A_{s}^{\kappa}}|\theta|(X_{s})\right]\lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}s).

Take any
\beta>p/(p-1)>1
and
\beta^{\prime}:=\beta/(\beta-1)\in]1,p[
. Then

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\left|Q_{t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\theta(x)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{s\geq 0}\mathbb{E}_{x}\left[e^{-\alpha s-A_{s}^{\kappa}}|\theta|(X_{s})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{s\geq 0}\mathbb{E}_{x}\left[e^{-\beta(\alpha s+A_{s}^{\kappa})}\right]^{\frac{1}{\beta}}\mathbb{E}_{x}\left[|\theta|^{\beta^{\prime}}(x)\right]^{\frac{1}{\beta^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{s\geq 0,y\in M}\left(P_{s}^{\beta(\alpha\mathfrak{m}+\kappa)}1(y)\right)^{\frac{1}{\beta}}\cdot\sup_{s\geq 0}\left(P_{s}|\theta|^{\beta^{\prime}}(x)\right)^{\frac{1}{\beta^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{s\geq 0,y\in M}\left(P_{s}^{\alpha\mathfrak{m}-\beta\kappa^{-}}1(y)\right)^{\frac{1}{\beta}}\cdot\sup_{s\geq 0}\left(P_{s}|\theta|^{\beta^{\prime}}(x)\right)^{\frac{1}{\beta^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69046pt\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:KatoCoincidence}}}{{\leq}}\sup_{s\geq 0}\left(Ce^{-\alpha s+C_{\kappa}s}\right)^{\frac{1}{\beta}}\cdot\sup_{s\geq 0}\left(P_{s}|\theta|^{\beta^{\prime}}(x)\right)^{\frac{1}{\beta^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\frac{1}{\beta}}\sup_{s\geq 0}\left(P_{s}|\theta|^{\beta^{\prime}}(x)\right)^{\frac{1}{\beta^{\prime}}},\quad(\alpha\geq C_{\kappa}).

Applying Lemma
8.1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{M}\sup_{t\geq 0}\left|Q_{t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\theta(x)\right|^{p}\mathfrak{m}(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle=C^{\frac{p}{\beta}}\int_{M}\left|\sup_{s\geq 0}P_{s}|\theta|^{\beta^{\prime}}(x)\right|^{\frac{p}{\beta^{\prime}}}\mathfrak{m}(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\frac{p}{\beta}}\left(\frac{\frac{p}{\beta^{\prime}}}{\frac{p}{\beta^{\prime}}-1}\right)^{\frac{p}{\beta^{\prime}}}\int_{M}|\theta(x)|^{\beta^{\prime}\frac{p}{\beta^{\prime}}}\mathfrak{m}(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle=C^{\frac{p}{\beta}}\left(\frac{p}{p-\beta^{\prime}}\right)^{\frac{p}{\beta^{\prime}}}\|\theta\|_{L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)}^{p}

\displaystyle=C^{\frac{p}{\beta}}\left(\frac{p(\beta-1)}{(p-1)\beta-p}\right)^{\frac{p(\beta-1)}{\beta}}\|\theta\|_{L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)}^{p}.

∎

Now we set
\eta=\eta(x,t):=Q_{t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\theta(x)
.
Let
\overline{\nabla}:=\left(\nabla,\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\right)
be the covariant derivative over
X\times\mathbb{R}
and

\nabla
the covariant derivative acting on
W^{1,2}(TM)
:

\displaystyle|\overline{\nabla}\eta(x,t)|^{2}:=\left|\nabla(Q_{t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\theta)^{\sharp}(x)\right|_{\rm HS}^{2}+\left|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}Q_{t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\theta(x)\right|^{2}=|\nabla\eta^{\sharp}(x,t)|_{\rm HS}^{2}+\left|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\eta(x,t)\right|^{2}.

9

Auxiliary Brownian motion

Let
(B_{t},\mathbb{P}_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rightarrow}}{{a}}})
be one-dimensional Brownian motion
starting at
a\in\mathbb{R}
with the generator
\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial a^{2}}
. We set

\widehat{M}:=M\times\mathbb{R}
,
\hat{x}:=(x,a)\in\widehat{M}
,

\widehat{X}_{t}:=(X_{t},B_{t})
,
t\geq 0
,
\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}:=\mathfrak{m}\otimes m

and
\mathbb{P}_{\hat{x}}:=\mathbb{P}_{x}\otimes\mathbb{P}_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rightarrow}}{{a}}}
.
Then
\widehat{\bf X}:=(\widehat{X}_{t},\mathbb{P}_{\hat{x}})
is an
\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}
-symmetric diffusion process on

\widehat{M}
with the (formal) generator
\Delta+\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial a^{2}}
, where
m
is one-dimensional Lebesgue measure. Let
\tau:=\inf\{t>0\mid B_{t}=0\}
be the first hitting time of
B_{t}
to
\{0\}
.
We denote the semigroup on
L^{p}(\widehat{M};\widehat{\mathfrak{m}})
associated with the diffusion process

(\widehat{X}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
by
(\widehat{P}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
and its generator by
\widehat{\Delta}_{p}
. We also denote the Dirichlet form on
L^{2}(\widehat{M};\widehat{\mathfrak{m}})
associated with
\widehat{\Delta}_{2}
by

(\widehat{\mathscr{E}},D(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}))
. That is,

\displaystyle D(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}):

\displaystyle=\left\{u\in L^{2}(\widehat{M};\widehat{\mathfrak{m}})\;\left|\;\lim_{t\to 0}\frac{1}{t}(u-\widehat{P}_{t}u,u)_{L^{2}(\widehat{M};\widehat{\mathfrak{m}})}<+\infty\right.\right\},

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathscr{E}}(u,v):

\displaystyle=\lim_{t\to 0}\frac{1}{t}(u-\widehat{P}_{t}u,v)_{L^{2}(\widehat{M};\widehat{\mathfrak{m}})}\qquad\text{ for }\quad u,v\in D(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}).

Since
(\widehat{\mathscr{E}},D(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}))
is associated to the

\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}
-symmetric Borel right process
\widehat{\bf X}
, it is quasi-regular by
Fitzsimmons
[
24
]
.
We need the following lemma:

Lemma 9.1
.

We have the following:

(1)

Let
N
be an
\widehat{\mathscr{E}}
-exceptional set. Then, for
m
-a.e.
a\in\mathbb{R}
,
N_{a}:=\{x\in M\mid(x,a)\in N\}
is an
\mathscr{E}
-exceptional set.

(2)

Let
(x,a)\mapsto u(x,a)
be an
\widehat{\mathscr{E}}
-quasi continuous function. Then, for
m
-a.e.
a\in\mathbb{R}
,

x\mapsto u(x,a)
is an
\mathscr{E}
-quasi continuous function.

Proof.

(1) is proved in
\widehat{\rm O}
kura
[
45
, Theorem 4.1(4)]
. We prove (2). Since the
\mathscr{E}
-quasi continuity is equivalent to the
\mathscr{E}
-q.e. fine continuity by

[
25
, Theorem 4.2.2 and Theorem 4.6.1]
, it suffices to prove that the fine continuity of
(x,a)\mapsto u(x,a)
with respect to

\widehat{\bf X}
implies the fine continuity of
x\mapsto u(x,a)
for each
a\in\mathbb{R}
with respect to
{\bf X}
.
This can be confirmed by that for each
a\in\mathbb{R}

\displaystyle t\mapsto u(\widehat{X}_{t})=u(X_{t},a)\;\text{ is right continuous }\;\mathbb{P}_{(x,a)}\text{-a.s.~equivalently }\;\mathbb{P}_{x}\text{-a.s.~for }\;x\in M

in view of
[
25
, Theorem A.2.7]
.
∎

Throughout this subsection, we assume
\kappa^{+}\in S_{D}({\bf X})
and
\kappa^{-}\in S_{E\!K}({\bf X})
.
We define
\widehat{\kappa}^{\,\pm}:=\kappa^{\pm}\otimes m
and
\widehat{\kappa}:=\widehat{\,\kappa}^{+}-\widehat{\,\kappa}^{-}
.
The associated positive continuous additive functional (PCAF in short)
\widehat{A}_{t}^{\;\widehat{\,\kappa}^{\pm}}

in Revuz correspondence under
\widehat{\bf X}

is given by
\widehat{A}_{t}^{\;\widehat{\kappa}^{\pm}}=A_{t}^{\kappa^{\pm}}
, in particular,
\widehat{\kappa}^{\,+}
(resp.
\widehat{\kappa}^{\,-}
)
is a smooth measure of Dynkin (resp. extended Kato) class with respect to
\widehat{\bf X}
, i.e.

\widehat{\kappa}^{\,+}\in S_{D}(\widehat{\bf X})
(resp.
\widehat{\kappa}^{\,-}\in S_{E\!K}(\widehat{\bf X})
).
Then we can define the following quadratic form

(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\;\widehat{\kappa}},D(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\;\widehat{\kappa}}))

on
L^{2}(\widehat{M};\widehat{\mathfrak{m}})
:

\displaystyle D(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\;\widehat{\kappa}}):=D(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}),\qquad\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\;\widehat{\kappa}}(u,v):=\widehat{\mathscr{E}}(u,v)+\langle\widehat{\kappa},\tilde{u}\tilde{v}\rangle\quad\text{ for }\quad u,v\in D(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\;\widehat{\kappa}}).

(9.1)

Here
\tilde{u}
denotes the
\widehat{\mathscr{E}}
-quasi-continuous
\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}
-version of
u
with respect to

(\widehat{\mathscr{E}},D(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}))
.
The strongly continuous semigroup
(\widehat{P}_{t}^{\;\widehat{\kappa}})_{t\geq 0}
on
L^{2}(\widehat{M};\widehat{\mathfrak{m}})
associated with the quadratic form

(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\;\widehat{\kappa}},D(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\;\widehat{\kappa}}))

is given by

\displaystyle\widehat{P}_{t}^{\widehat{\;\kappa}}u(\hat{x})=\mathbb{E}_{\hat{x}}[e^{-A_{t}^{\kappa}}u(\widehat{X}_{t})]\quad\text{ for }\quad u\in L^{2}(\widehat{M};\widehat{\mathfrak{m}})\cap\mathscr{B}(\widehat{M}).

(9.2)

We denote by
\widehat{\mathscr{C}}:={\rm Test}(M)\odot C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
the totality of all finite linear combinations of
f\otimes\varphi
,
f\in{\rm Test}(M)
,
\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
, where

(f\otimes\varphi)(y):=f(x)\varphi(a)
. Meanwhile, the space
L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
and

D(\mathscr{E})\otimes H^{1,2}(\mathbb{R})
are usual tensor products of Hilbert spaces, where
H^{1,2}(\mathbb{R})
is the Sobolev space which consists of all functions
\varphi\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
such that the weak derivative
\dot{\varphi}
exists and belongs to
L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
. Then we have

Lemma 9.2

(Lemma 3.1 in
[
23
]
)
.

\widehat{\mathscr{C}}
is dense in
D(\widehat{\mathscr{E}})
. Moreover,
for
u,v\in D(\mathscr{E})\otimes H^{1,2}(\mathbb{R})
, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathscr{E}}(u,v)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathscr{E}(u(\cdot,a),v(\cdot,a))m(\mathrm{d}a)+\int_{M}\mathfrak{m}(\mathrm{d}x)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\partial u}{\partial a}(x,a)\frac{\partial v}{\partial a}(x,a)m(\mathrm{d}a).

(9.3)

The following lemma is proved in
[
50
, Proposition 3.9]
:

Lemma 9.3

(
[
50
, Proposition 3.9]
)
.

Let
h
be a non-negative measurable function on
[0,+\infty[
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rightarrow}}{{a}}}\left[\int_{0}^{\tau}h(B_{s})\mathrm{d}s\right]=\int_{0}^{\infty}(a\land t)h(t)\mathrm{d}t.

(9.4)

Based on Lemma
9.3
, we can obtain the following generalization of
[
49
, Proposition 6.1]

Proposition 9.4

(
[
49
, Proposition 6.1]
)
.

Let
\nu
be a non-negative smooth measure on
M
associated with a PCAF
A^{\nu}
in Revuz correspondence
under
{\bf X}

and
j
be a non-negative
measurable function on
X\times[0,+\infty[
. Denote by
\widehat{A}^{\,\widehat{\nu}}
the PCAF of
\widehat{\bf X}
associated with the smooth measure
\widehat{\nu}:=\nu\otimes m
with respect to
\widehat{\bf X}
.
Then for all
a\in]0,+\infty[
and Borel measurable non-negative function
j
on
X\times\mathbb{R}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{m}\otimes\delta_{a}}\left[\int_{0}^{\tau}j(\widehat{X}_{s})\mathrm{d}\widehat{A}_{s}^{\,\widehat{\nu}}\right]=\int_{M}\int_{0}^{\infty}(a\land t)j(x,t)\nu(\mathrm{d}x)\mathrm{d}t.

(9.5)

Let
{\rm Reg}(T^{*}\!M)\odot C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
be the algebraic tensor product of
{\rm Reg}(T^{*}\!M)
and
C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
, i.e., any element of
{\rm Reg}(T^{*}\!M)\odot C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
forms a finite linear combination of
\theta\otimes\varphi
with

\theta\in{\rm Reg}(T^{*}\!M)
and
\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
,
which is a dense subspace of the tensor product
L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
of Hilbert spaces
L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)
and
L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
.
We now define the quadratic form
\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\,\rm HK}
on
{\rm Reg}(T^{*}\!M)\odot C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
by

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\rm HK}(\theta,\eta):=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathscr{E}^{\rm HK}(\theta(\cdot,t),\omega(\cdot,t))\mathrm{d}t+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\langle\dot{\theta}(\cdot,t),\dot{\eta}(\cdot,t)\rangle_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)}\mathrm{d}t,\quad\theta,\eta\in{\rm Reg}(T^{*}\!M)\odot C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}).

Lemma 9.5
.

The quadratic form
(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\,\rm HK},{\rm Reg}(T^{*}\!M)\odot C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}))
is closable on
L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
. Denote by
(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\,\rm HK},D(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\,\rm HK}))
its closure on
L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
.
Then,
(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\,\rm HK},D(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\,\rm HK}))
is a closed bilinear form on
L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
.

Proof.

For
\theta\in{\rm Reg}(T^{*}\!M)\odot C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
, it is easy to see
\widehat{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm HK}\theta:=\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}+{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK}\right)\theta\in L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
. Then

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\,\rm HK}(\theta,\eta)=\left(-\widehat{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm HK}\theta,\eta\right)_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\quad\text{ for }\quad\theta,\eta\in{\rm Reg}(T^{*}\!M)\odot C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}).

Then the closability of
(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\,\rm HK},D(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\,\rm HK}))
on
L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
follows

[
43
, Chapter I, Proposition 3.3]
.
∎

Remark 9.6
.

The norm
\|\cdot\|_{H^{1,2}(T^{*}\!M)\otimes H^{1,2}(\mathbb{R})}
of the tensor product
H^{1,2}(T^{*}\!M)\otimes H^{1,2}(\mathbb{R})
of Hilbert spaces
H^{1,2}(T^{*}\!M)
and
H^{1,2}(\mathbb{R})
does not coincide with the norm
\|\cdot\|_{\widehat{\mathscr{E}}_{1}^{\,\rm HK}}
derived from

(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\,\rm HK},D(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\,\rm HK}))
on
L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
. Indeed, for
\theta\in{\rm Reg}(T^{*}\!M)
and
\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
.

\displaystyle\|\theta\otimes\varphi\|_{H^{1,2}(T^{*}\!M)\otimes H^{1,2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|\theta\|_{H^{1,2}(T^{*}\!M)}^{2}\|\varphi\|_{H^{1,2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\mathscr{E}^{\rm HK}(\theta,\theta)+\|\theta\|_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)}^{2}\right)\left(\|\dot{\varphi}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}+\|\varphi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\|\theta\otimes\varphi\|_{\widehat{\mathscr{E}}_{1}^{\,\rm HK}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\,\rm HK}_{1}(\theta\otimes\varphi,\theta\otimes\varphi)

\displaystyle=\mathscr{E}^{\rm HK}(\theta,\theta)\|\varphi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}+\|\theta\|_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)}^{2}\|\dot{\varphi}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}+\|\theta\|_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)}^{2}\|\varphi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}.

Lemma 9.7
.

We have the inclusion
H^{1,2}(T^{*}\!M)\odot H^{1,2}(\mathbb{R})\subset D(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\rm HK})
. The map

H^{1,2}(T^{*}\!M)\times H^{1,2}(\mathbb{R})\to H^{1,2}(T^{*}\!M)\odot H^{1,2}(\mathbb{R})
is continuous.

Proof.

It suffices to show that
\theta\otimes\varphi\in D(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\rm HK})
for
\theta\in H^{1,2}(T^{*}\!M)
and
\varphi H^{1,2}(\mathbb{R})
. Take an approximating sequence
\{\theta_{n}\}\subset{\rm Reg}(T^{*}\!M)
(resp.
\{\varphi_{n}\}\subset C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
) in
H^{1,2}(T^{*}\!M)
(resp. in
H^{1,2}(\mathbb{R})
) to
\theta\in H^{1,2}(T^{*}\!M)
(resp.
\varphi\in H^{1,2}(\mathbb{R})
).
Then it is easy to see that
\{\theta_{n}\otimes\varphi_{n}\}\subset H^{1,2}(T^{*}\!M)\odot H^{1,2}(\mathbb{R})
is an

\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\,\rm HK}_{1}
-Cauchy sequence. This yields the conclusion and the following identity: for
\theta,\eta\in H^{1,2}(T^{*}\!M)
and
\varphi,\psi\in H^{1,2}(\mathbb{R})
,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\,\rm HK}(\theta\otimes\varphi,\eta\otimes\psi)=\mathscr{E}^{\rm HK}(\theta,\eta)(\varphi,\psi)_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}+\langle\theta,\eta\rangle_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)}(\dot{\varphi},\dot{\psi})_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})},

which implies the desired continuity.
∎

Lemma 9.8
.

Suppose
\eta\in L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
. Then the following are equivalent to each other:

(1)

\eta\in D(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\,\rm HK})
.

(2)

For a.e.
t\in\mathbb{R}
, we have
\eta(\cdot,t)\in D({\mathscr{E}}^{\,\rm HK})
and
\dot{\eta}(\cdot,t)\in L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)
. Moreover,
\int_{\mathbb{R}}{\mathscr{E}}^{\,\rm HK}_{1}(\eta(\cdot,t),\eta(\cdot,t))\mathrm{d}t<\infty
and
\int_{\mathbb{R}}\|\dot{\eta}(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)}^{2}\mathrm{d}t<\infty
.

Here
\dot{\eta}(\cdot,t)
denotes the
L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)
-valued
distributional derivative of
\eta(\cdot,t)
with respect to
t\in\mathbb{R}

Proof.

(1)
\Longrightarrow
(2): Suppose
\eta\in D(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\,\rm HK})
. Let
\eta_{n}\in{\rm Reg}(T^{*}\!M)\odot C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
be an
\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\,\rm HK}_{1}
-approximating sequence to
\eta
. By taking a subsequence if necessary we may assume

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}2^{k}\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\,\rm HK}_{1}(\eta_{k+1}-\eta_{k},\eta_{k+1}-\eta_{k})<\infty.

(9.6)

Then, for each
T\in]0,+\infty[
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\mathscr{E}^{\,\rm HK}_{1}(\eta_{k+1}(\cdot,t)-\eta_{k}(\cdot,t),\eta_{k+1}(\cdot,t)-\eta_{k}(\cdot,t))^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\sqrt{T}2^{-\frac{k}{2}}\left(2^{k}\int_{0}^{T}{\mathscr{E}}^{\,\rm HK}_{1}(\eta_{k+1}(\cdot,t)-\eta_{k}(\cdot,t),\eta_{k+1}(\cdot,t)-\eta_{k}(\cdot,t))\mathrm{d}t\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{T}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}2^{-k}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}2^{k}\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\,\rm HK}_{1}(\eta_{k+1}-\eta_{k},\eta_{k+1}-\eta_{k})\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}<\infty.

Thus,

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\mathscr{E}^{\rm HK}_{1}(\eta_{k+1}(\cdot,t)-\eta_{k}(\cdot,t),\eta_{k+1}(\cdot,t)-\eta_{k}(\cdot,t))^{\frac{1}{2}}<\infty\quad\text{ for\;\; a.e. }\quad t\in\mathbb{R}.

This means that
\{\eta_{n}(\cdot,t)\}
is an
\mathscr{E}^{\,\rm HK}_{1}
-Cauchy sequence for a.e.
t\in\mathbb{R}
. On the other hand,
by taking a further subsequence,

\{\eta_{n}(\cdot,t)\}
converges to
\eta(\cdot,t)
in
L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)
for a.e.
t\in\mathbb{R}
. Therefore,
\eta(\cdot,t)\in H^{1,2}(T^{*}\!M)=D(\mathscr{E}^{\,\rm HK})
and
\eta_{n}(\cdot,t)\to\eta(\cdot,t)
in
H^{1,2}(T^{*}\!M)
for a.e.
t\in\mathbb{R}
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathscr{E}^{\rm HK}_{1}(\eta(\cdot,t),\eta(\cdot,t))\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathscr{E}^{\rm HK}_{1}(\eta_{n}(\cdot,t),\eta_{n}(\cdot,t))\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\varliminf_{n\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathscr{E}^{\rm HK}_{1}(\eta_{n}(\cdot,t),\eta_{n}(\cdot,t))\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\,\rm HK}_{1}(\eta_{n},\eta_{n})=\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\,\rm HK}_{1}(\eta,\eta)<\infty.

From (
9.6
), we can similarly deduce

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\|\dot{\eta}_{k+1}(\cdot,t)-\dot{\eta}(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)}<\infty\quad\text{ for\;\; a.e. }\quad t\in\mathbb{R}

as above.
Hence
\{\dot{\eta}_{n}(\cdot,t)\}
is an
L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)
-Cauchy sequence for a.e.
t\in\mathbb{R}
. Let
v(\cdot,t)
be its limit in

L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)
. By using an integration by parts, we see for
\theta\in{\rm Reg}(T^{*}\!M)
and
\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})

\displaystyle\langle v,\theta\otimes\varphi\rangle_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\lim_{n\to\infty}\langle\dot{\eta}_{n}(\cdot,t),\theta\rangle_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)}\varphi(t)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=-\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\langle\eta_{n}(\cdot,t),\theta\rangle_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)}\dot{\varphi}(t)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\langle\eta(\cdot,t),\theta\rangle_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)}\dot{\varphi}(t)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\left\langle-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta(\cdot,t)\dot{\varphi}(t)\mathrm{d}t,\theta\right\rangle_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)}=\left\langle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\dot{\eta}(\cdot,t){\varphi}(t)\mathrm{d}t,\theta\right\rangle_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)}

\displaystyle=\langle\dot{\eta},\theta\otimes\varphi\rangle_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{R})},

hence
v(\cdot,t)=\dot{\eta}(\cdot,t)
for a.e.
t\in\mathbb{R}
. Therefore

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\|\dot{\eta}(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)}^{2}+\|{\eta}(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)}^{2})\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\lim_{n\to\infty}(\|\dot{\eta}_{n}(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)}^{2}+\|{\eta}_{n}(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)}^{2})\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\varliminf_{n\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\|\dot{\eta}_{n}(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)}^{2}+\|{\eta}_{n}(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)}^{2})\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{n\to\infty}\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\,\rm HK}(\eta_{n},\eta_{n})=\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\,\rm HK}(\eta,\eta)<\infty.

(2)
\Longrightarrow
(1): Suppose the conditions in (2) for
\eta\in L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
.
Let
(\widehat{P}_{t}^{\,\rm HK})
be the semigroup on
L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
associated to
the quadratic form
(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\,\rm HK},D(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\,\rm HK}))
on
L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
.
Then we see

\displaystyle\widehat{P}_{t}^{\,\rm HK}\eta(x,s)={P}_{t}^{\,\rm HK}\left(P_{t}^{w}\eta(\cdot,s)\right)(x)\quad\text{ for }\quad\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}\text{-a.e.~}(x,s),

(9.7)

where

\displaystyle P_{t}^{w}\eta(\cdot,s):=\frac{1}{\sqrt{4\pi t}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-\frac{(u-s)^{2}}{4\pi t}}\eta(\cdot,u)\mathrm{d}u

is defined to be a Bochner integral on
L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)
. Similarly, we can define
P_{t}^{w}\mathrm{d}\eta(\cdot,s)
and

P_{t}^{w}\mathrm{d}^{*}\eta(\cdot,s)
. Then

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\,\rm HK}

\displaystyle(\widehat{P}_{t}^{\,\rm HK}\eta,\widehat{P}_{t}^{\,\rm HK}\eta)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}{\mathscr{E}}^{\rm HK}({P}_{t}^{\,\rm HK}P_{t}^{w}\eta(\cdot,s),{P}_{t}^{\,\rm HK}P_{t}^{w}\eta(\cdot,s))\mathrm{d}s+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|P_{t}^{\rm HK}\frac{\partial}{\partial s}P_{t}^{w}\eta(\cdot,s)\right|_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)}^{2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}{\mathscr{E}}^{\rm HK}(P_{t}^{w}\eta(\cdot,s),P_{t}^{w}\eta(\cdot,s))\mathrm{d}s+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\frac{\partial}{\partial s}P_{t}^{w}\eta(\cdot,s)\right|_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)}^{2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}{\mathscr{E}}^{\rm HK}(\eta(\cdot,s),\eta(\cdot,s))\mathrm{d}s+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\frac{\partial}{\partial s}\eta(\cdot,s)\right|_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)}^{2}\mathrm{d}s<\infty.

In the last inequality, we use
|P_{t}^{w}\mathrm{d}\eta|\leq P_{t}^{w}|\mathrm{d}\eta|
,
|P_{t}^{w}\mathrm{d}^{*}\eta|\leq P_{t}^{w}|\mathrm{d}^{*}\eta|
, and
\frac{\partial}{\partial s}P_{t}^{w}\eta(\cdot,s)=P_{t}^{w}\frac{\partial}{\partial s}\eta(\cdot,s)
for
\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}
-a.e.
(x,s)
. Thus we have
(\widehat{P}_{t}^{\,\rm HK}\eta)_{t\geq 0}
is
\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\,\rm HK}_{1}
-bounded.
Therfore,
\eta\in D(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\,\rm HK})
.
∎

Theorem 9.9
.

Take
\omega=\omega(x,t)\in D(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\,\rm HK})
. Then
|\omega|\in D(\widehat{\mathscr{E}})
and

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\,\widehat{\kappa}}(|\omega|,|\omega|)\leq\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\,\rm HK}(\omega,\omega).

Proof.

Applying the Fatou’s lemma for Dirichlet form on
L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
associated to the one-dimensional Brownian motion
(B_{t})_{t\geq 0}

(see
[
47
]
for Fatou’s lemma for Dirichlet forms),

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\frac{\partial|\omega|}{\partial t}\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\varliminf_{n\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\frac{\partial(|\omega|^{2}+\frac{1}{n^{2}})^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\partial t}\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\varliminf_{n\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\langle\omega,\dot{\omega}\rangle^{2}}{|\omega|^{2}+\frac{1}{n^{2}}}\mathrm{d}t\leq\varliminf_{n\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{|\omega|^{2}|\dot{\omega}|^{2}}{|\omega|^{2}+\frac{1}{n^{2}}}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\dot{\omega}|^{2}\mathrm{d}t.

(9.8)

On the other hand, Hess-Schrader-Uhlenbrock inequality (
6.6
) tells us the following (see
[
39
]
)

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{\kappa}(|\omega|(\cdot,t),|\omega|(\cdot,t))\leq\mathscr{E}^{\rm HK}(\omega(\cdot,t),\omega(\cdot,t)).

(9.9)

Combining (
9.8
) and (
9.9
), we have

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\,\widehat{\kappa}}(|\omega|,|\omega|)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathscr{E}^{\kappa}(|\omega|(\cdot,t),|\omega|(\cdot,t))\mathrm{d}t+\int_{M}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\frac{\partial|\omega|}{\partial t}\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{m}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathscr{E}^{\rm HK}(\omega(\cdot,t),\omega(\cdot,t))\mathrm{d}t+\int_{M}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\dot{\omega}\right|^{2}\mathfrak{m}(\mathrm{d}x)\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{m}=\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\rm HK}(\omega,\omega).

∎

Corollary 9.10
.

Let
\eta=\eta(x,t):=Q_{|t|}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\theta(x)
with
\theta\in D({\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK})\cap L^{\infty}(T^{*}\!M)
and
\alpha>C_{\kappa}
.
Then
\eta\in D(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\,\rm HK})
, hence

|\eta|\in D(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\,\widehat{\kappa}})\cap L^{\infty}(\widehat{M};\widehat{\mathfrak{m}})=D(\widehat{\mathscr{E}})\cap L^{\infty}(\widehat{M};\widehat{\mathfrak{m}})
.
In particular,
|\eta|^{2}\in D(\widehat{\mathscr{E}})\cap L^{\infty}(\widehat{M};\widehat{\mathfrak{m}})
.
Moreover, for
p\in]0,+\infty[
and
\varepsilon>0
,
\eta_{{\varepsilon}}:=(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}-{\varepsilon}^{p}
satisfies
\eta_{{\varepsilon}}\in D(\widehat{\mathscr{E}})\cap L^{\infty}(\widehat{M};\widehat{\mathfrak{m}})
.

Proof.

From (
8.1
), we easily see

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\|\eta(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)}^{2}\mathrm{d}t\leq C^{2}\|\theta\|_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-2\sqrt{\alpha-C_{\kappa}}|t|}\mathrm{d}t<\infty,

hence
\eta\in L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
.
For a fixed
t>0
, we see

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{\rm HK}_{\alpha}(\eta(\cdot,t),\eta(\cdot,t))

\displaystyle=((\alpha-{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK})\eta(\cdot,t),\eta(\cdot,t))_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)}

\displaystyle=\|\sqrt{\alpha-{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK}}\eta(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)}

\displaystyle=\int_{M}|\dot{\eta}(\cdot,t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{m},

where we use
\dot{\eta}=\sqrt{\alpha-{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK}}\eta
in
L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)
.
Applying (
8.1
) again,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\|(\alpha-{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK})\eta(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)}^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\|Q_{|t|}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}(\alpha-{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK})\theta\|_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)}^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\|(\alpha-{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK})\theta\|_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)}^{2}C^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-2\sqrt{\alpha-C_{\kappa}}|t|}\mathrm{d}t<\infty.

Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\,\rm HK}_{\alpha}(\eta,\eta)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}{\mathscr{E}}^{\,\rm HK}_{\alpha}(\eta(\cdot,t),\eta(\cdot,t))\mathrm{d}t+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\|\dot{\eta}(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)}^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=2\int_{\mathbb{R}}{\mathscr{E}}^{\,\rm HK}_{\alpha}(\eta(\cdot,t),\eta(\cdot,t))\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{\int_{\mathbb{R}}\|(\alpha-{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK})\eta(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)}^{2}\mathrm{d}t}\sqrt{\int_{\mathbb{R}}\|\eta(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)}^{2}\mathrm{d}t}<\infty.

By Lemma
9.8
, this inequality shows
\eta\in D(\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\,\rm HK})
.
Theorem
9.9
also shows
|\eta|\in D(\widehat{\mathscr{E}})\cap L^{\infty}(\widehat{M};\widehat{\mathfrak{m}})
.
The final assertion follows from the fact that
T(x):=\Psi(|x|)=(|x|+{\varepsilon}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}-{\varepsilon}^{p}
satisfies
T(0)=0
and

|T(x)-T(y)|\leq\frac{p}{2}{\varepsilon}^{p-2}|x-y|
for
x,y\in\mathbb{R}
(see
[
25
,
(\mathscr{E},4)^{\prime\prime}
]
).
∎

By Corollary
9.10
, we can apply Fukushima’s decomposition to

|\eta|^{2}
for
\eta=Q_{|t|}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\theta
with

\theta\in D({\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK})\cap L^{\infty}(T^{*}\!M)
and
\alpha>C_{\kappa}
with respect to
\widehat{\bf X}
.
That is, there exists
a martingale additive functional of finite energy
M^{[|\eta|^{2}]}
and a continuous additive functional of zero energy
N^{[v]}
such that

\displaystyle\widetilde{|\eta|^{2}}(\widehat{X}_{t})-\widetilde{|\eta|^{2}}(\widehat{X}_{0})=\widehat{M}_{t}^{[|\eta|^{2}]}+\widehat{N}_{t}^{[|\eta|^{2}]}\quad t\geq 0\quad\mathbb{P}_{\hat{x}}\text{-a.s.~for }\widehat{\mathscr{E}}\text{-q.e.~}\hat{x},

(9.10)

where
\widetilde{|\eta|^{2}}
is an
\widehat{\mathscr{E}}
-quasi-continuous
\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}
-version of
|\eta|^{2}
, where

\eta=Q_{|t|}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\theta
with

\theta\in D({\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK})\cap L^{\infty}(T^{*}\!M)
.
See
[
25
, Theorem 5.2.2]
for Fukushima’s decomposition theorem. Thanks to
[
25
, Theorem 5.2.3]
, we know that

\displaystyle\langle\widehat{M}^{[|\eta|^{2}]}\rangle_{t}=\int_{0}^{t}\left\{\left|\nabla|\eta|^{2}\right|^{2}(\widehat{X}_{s})+\left(\frac{\partial|\eta|^{2}}{\partial a}(\widehat{X}_{s})\right)^{2}\right\}\mathrm{d}s,\quad t\geq 0.

(9.11)

See also
[
25
, Theorem 5.1.3 and Example 5.2.1]
for details.
Similarly, for
\eta_{{\varepsilon}}=\Psi(|\eta|^{2})=(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}-{\varepsilon}^{p}\in D(\widehat{\mathscr{E}})
with
p\in]0,+\infty[
we can consider

\displaystyle\widetilde{\eta}_{{\varepsilon}}(\widehat{X}_{t})-\widetilde{\eta}_{{\varepsilon}}(\widehat{X}_{0})=\widehat{M}_{t}^{[\eta_{{\varepsilon}}]}+\widehat{N}_{t}^{[\eta_{{\varepsilon}}]}\quad t\geq 0\quad\mathbb{P}_{\hat{x}}\text{-a.s.~for }\widehat{\mathscr{E}}\text{-q.e.~}\hat{x}

(9.12)

with respect to
\widehat{\bf X}
.

10

Expressions for the CAFs
\widehat{N}_{t}^{[|\eta|^{2}]}
and
\widehat{N}_{t}^{[\eta_{{\varepsilon}}]}
of zero energy

Lemma 10.1

(Bochner formula)
.

Let
\eta\in D({\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK})\cap L^{\infty}{(T^{*}\!M)}
.
Then
|\eta|^{2}\in D_{f}(\text{\boldmath$\Delta$})_{+}
with

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\text{\boldmath$\Delta$}|\eta|^{2}=\left[\langle\eta,{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK}\eta\rangle+|\nabla\eta^{\sharp}|_{\rm HS}^{2}\right]\mathfrak{m}+{\bf Ric}(\eta^{\sharp},\eta^{\sharp}).

(10.1)

Proof.

Since
\eta\in D({\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK})\cap L^{\infty}(T^{*}\!M)\subset H^{1,2}(T^{*}\!M)\cap L^{\infty}(T^{*}\!M)
,
|\eta|\in D(\mathscr{E})\cap L^{\infty}(M;\mathfrak{m})
is proved in
[
39
]
, hence
|\eta|^{2}\in D(\mathscr{E})\cap L^{\infty}(M;\mathfrak{m})
.
When
\eta\in{\rm Reg}(T^{*}\!M)
, by
[
12
, Lemma 8.2]
,
|\eta|^{2}\in D(\text{\boldmath$\Delta$}^{2\kappa})
, then

|\eta|^{2}\in D(\text{\boldmath$\Delta$})
by Lemma
4.19
. In this case, we have (
10.1
)
by (
7.6
). The expression (
10.1
) yields
|\eta|^{2}\in D_{f}(\text{\boldmath$\Delta$}^{2\kappa})
,
i.e.,
\text{\boldmath$\Delta$}^{2\kappa}|\eta|^{2}
is a finite signed smooth measure, because the right-hand-side of
(
10.1
) is a signed finite smooth measure for any
\eta\in D({\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK})
by Theorem
7.5
and

|\eta|\in D(\mathscr{E})
implies
\int_{M}\widetilde{|\eta|^{2}}\mathrm{d}|\kappa|<\infty
. Therefore, we have
|\eta|^{2}\in D_{f}(\text{\boldmath$\Delta$})

by Lemma
4.19
and (
10.1
). This implies that for any
\phi\in D(\mathscr{E})
and
\eta\in{\rm Reg}(T^{*}\!M)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\mathscr{E}^{2\kappa}(\phi,|\eta|^{2})=\int_{M}\phi\left[\langle\eta,{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK}\eta\rangle+|\nabla\eta^{\sharp}|_{\rm HS}^{2}\right]\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{m}+\int_{M}\tilde{\phi}\,\mathrm{d}\,{\bf Ric}^{\kappa}(\eta^{\sharp},\eta^{\sharp}),

(10.2)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\mathscr{E}(\phi,|\eta|^{2})=\int_{M}\phi\left[\langle\eta,{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK}\eta\rangle+|\nabla\eta^{\sharp}|_{\rm HS}^{2}\right]\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{m}+\int_{M}\tilde{\phi}\,\mathrm{d}\,{\bf Ric}(\eta^{\sharp},\eta^{\sharp}).

(10.3)

Finally we prove the assertion
|\eta|^{2}\in D_{f}(\text{\boldmath$\Delta$})
with (
10.1
) for the case

\eta=Q_{t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\theta
,
\theta\in D({\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK})\cap L^{\infty}(T^{*}\!M)
.
Let
\{\eta_{n}\}\subset{\rm Reg}(T^{*}\!M)(\subset L^{\infty}(T^{*}\!M))
be an
H^{1,2}(T^{*}\!M)
-approximating sequence to

\eta
satisfying
\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\|\eta_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(T^{*}\!M)}<\infty
. Fix
\phi\in{\rm Test}(M)
.
It is easy to see that
\{|\eta_{n}|^{2}\}(\subset D(\mathscr{E}))

L^{2}
-strongly converges to
|\eta|^{2}\in D(\mathscr{E})
.
We can prove that
\{|\eta_{n}|^{2}\}(\subset D(\mathscr{E}))
is
\mathscr{E}_{1}
-weakly (and
\mathscr{E}
-weakly) convergent to
|\eta|^{2}\in D(\mathscr{E})
. Indeed, for
\phi\in{\rm Test}(M)
, we can easily get that
\{\mathscr{E}_{1}(|\eta_{n}|^{2},\phi)\}
and
\{\mathscr{E}(|\eta_{n}|^{2},\phi)\}
are Cauchy sequences
for such
\eta_{n}\in{\rm Reg}(T^{*}\!M)
.
Since
{\rm Test}(M)
is
\mathscr{E}_{1}^{1/2}
-dense in
D(\mathscr{E})
, they are
Cauchy sequences and converges to
\{\mathscr{E}_{1}(|\eta|^{2},\phi)\}
and
\{\mathscr{E}(|\eta|^{2},\phi)\}

for any
\phi\in D(\mathscr{E})
, respectively. Thus, we have the expressions (
10.2
) and (
10.3
) for
\phi\in D(\mathscr{E})
. Therefore,
(
10.3
) for
\phi\in D(\mathscr{E})
implies
|\eta|^{2}\in D_{f}(\text{\boldmath$\Delta$})
and (
10.1
).
∎

Corollary 10.2
.

Let
\eta=Q_{t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\theta
with
\theta\in D({\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK})\cap L^{\infty}(T^{*}\!M)
. Then, for
\alpha\geq C_{\kappa}

\displaystyle\frac{\;1\;}{2}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}+\text{\boldmath$\Delta$}\right)|\eta|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left[\alpha|\eta|^{2}+\left|\overline{\nabla}\eta\right|^{2}\right]\mathfrak{m}+{\bf Ric}(\eta^{\sharp},\eta^{\sharp}),

(10.4)

\displaystyle\frac{\;1\;}{2}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}+\Delta_{\ll}\right)|\eta|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left[\alpha|\eta|^{2}+\left|\overline{\nabla}\eta\right|^{2}\right]+{\mathfrak{Ric}}(\eta^{\sharp},\eta^{\sharp})\quad\mathfrak{m}\text{-a.e.}

(10.5)

Here the left hand side of (
10.4
) is understood as

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}+\text{\boldmath$\Delta$}\right)|\eta|^{2}:=\frac{\partial^{2}|\eta|^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}\mathfrak{m}+\text{\boldmath$\Delta$}|\eta|^{2}.

(10.6)

Proof.

We calculate

\displaystyle\frac{\,1\,}{2}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}|\eta|^{2}=|\dot{\eta}|^{2}+\langle\eta,\dot{\eta}\rangle=|\dot{\eta}|^{2}+\langle\eta,(\alpha-{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK})\eta\rangle,

(10.7)

because
\eta=Q_{t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\theta
is the solution of

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}\eta(x,t)=(\alpha-{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK})\eta(x,t)

in
L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})
for
\alpha\geq C_{\kappa}
.
Adding (
10.7
)
\times\mathfrak{m}
to (
10.1
), we obtain (
10.4
) and (
10.5
).
∎

Theorem 10.3
.

Let
\eta=Q_{t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\theta
with
\theta\in D({\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK})\cap L^{\infty}(T^{*}\!M)
and
\alpha>C_{\kappa}
.
The CAF of zero energy part
\widehat{N}_{t}^{[|\eta|^{2}]}
appeared in the Fukushima’s decomposition (
9.10
)
is given by

\displaystyle\widehat{N}_{t}^{[|\eta|^{2}]}=2\int_{0}^{t}(\alpha|\eta|^{2}+|\overline{\nabla}\eta|^{2})(\widehat{X}_{s})\mathrm{d}s+2\widehat{A}_{t}^{\,{\bf Ric}(\eta^{\sharp},\eta^{\sharp})\otimes m}.

(10.8)

Here
\widehat{A}_{t}^{\,{\bf Ric}(\eta^{\sharp},\eta^{\sharp})\otimes m}
is the PCAF associated with the smooth measure

{\bf Ric}(\eta^{\sharp},\eta^{\sharp})\otimes m
with respect to
\widehat{\bf X}
.
Moreover, for
\eta_{{\varepsilon}}=(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}-{\varepsilon}^{p}
with
p\in]0,+\infty[

\displaystyle\widehat{N}_{t}^{[\eta_{{\varepsilon}}]}

\displaystyle=p\int_{0}^{t}\left(|\eta|^{2}(\widehat{X}_{s})+{\varepsilon}^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}-1}\left(\alpha|\eta|^{2}(\widehat{X}_{s})+|\overline{\nabla}\eta|^{2}(\widehat{X}_{s})\right)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+p\int_{0}^{t}\left(|\eta|^{2}(\widehat{X}_{s})+{\varepsilon}^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}-1}\mathrm{d}\widehat{A}_{s}^{\,{\bf Ric}(\eta^{\sharp},\eta^{\sharp})\otimes m}

(10.9)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\frac{p}{4}(p-2)\int_{0}^{t}\left(|\eta|^{2}(\widehat{X}_{s})+{\varepsilon}^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}-2}\left(\left|\nabla|\eta|^{2}\right|^{2}(\widehat{X}_{s})+\left|\frac{\partial|\eta|^{2}}{\partial a}(\widehat{X}_{s})\right|^{2}\right)\mathrm{d}s.

Remark 10.4
.

Thanks to (
10.4
) and (
10.12
) below,
(
10.8
) and (
10.9
) possesses the following formal expressions:

\displaystyle\widehat{N}_{t}^{[|\eta|^{2}]}

\displaystyle=\widehat{A}_{t}^{\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}+\text{\boldmath$\Delta$}\right)|\eta|^{2}}=\text{``}\int_{0}^{t}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}+\text{\boldmath$\Delta$}\right)|\eta|^{2}(X_{s})\mathrm{d}s\text{''},

\displaystyle\widehat{N}_{t}^{[\eta_{{\varepsilon}}]}

\displaystyle=\widehat{A}_{t}^{\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}+\text{\boldmath$\Delta$}\right)\eta_{{\varepsilon}}}=\text{``}\int_{0}^{t}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}+\text{\boldmath$\Delta$}\right)\eta_{{\varepsilon}}(X_{s})\mathrm{d}s\text{''}.

N_{t}^{[|\eta|^{2}]}
may not be a PCAF (equivalently
|\eta|^{2}(\widehat{X}_{t})

may not be a submartingale) unless
\kappa^{-}=0
.

Theorem
10.3
.

Take
\phi\in D(\mathscr{E})
and
\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
. By way of expression (
10.2
) for

\phi\in D(\mathscr{E})
and
\eta\in D({\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK})\cap L^{\infty}(T^{*}\!M)
, we have

\displaystyle-\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\,2\widehat{\kappa}}(|\eta|^{2},\phi\otimes\varphi)

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathscr{E}^{2\kappa}(|\eta|^{2},\phi)\varphi(t)\mathrm{d}t-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left\langle\frac{\partial|\eta|^{2}}{\partial t},\phi\right\rangle_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)}\dot{\varphi}(t)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi(t)\int_{M}\tilde{\phi}\,\mathrm{d}\text{\boldmath$\Delta$}^{2\kappa}|\eta|^{2}\mathrm{d}t+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi(t)\int_{M}\phi\frac{\partial^{2}|\eta|^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{m}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi(t)\int_{M}\tilde{\phi}\,\mathrm{d}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}+\text{\boldmath$\Delta$}^{2\kappa}\right)|\eta|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi\left\langle\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}+\text{\boldmath$\Delta$}^{2\kappa}\right)|\eta|^{2},\phi\right\rangle_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)}\mathrm{d}t.

By (
10.4
), we have

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{\mathscr{E}}^{\,2\widehat{\kappa}}(|\eta|^{2},\phi\otimes\varphi)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi\left\langle(\alpha|\eta|^{2}+|\overline{\nabla}\eta|^{2})\mathfrak{m}+{\bf Ric}(\eta^{\sharp},\eta^{\sharp})-\widetilde{|\eta|^{2}}\kappa,\phi\right\rangle\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\left\langle(\alpha|\eta|^{2}+|\overline{\nabla}\eta|^{2})\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}+{\bf Ric}^{\kappa}(\eta^{\sharp},\eta^{\sharp})\otimes m,\phi\otimes\varphi\right\rangle

and

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{\mathscr{E}}(|\eta|^{2},\phi\otimes\varphi)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi\left\langle(\alpha|\eta|^{2}+|\overline{\nabla}\eta|^{2})\mathfrak{m}+{\bf Ric}(\eta^{\sharp},\eta^{\sharp}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\left\langle(\alpha|\eta|^{2}+|\overline{\nabla}\eta|^{2})\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}+{\bf Ric}(\eta^{\sharp},\eta^{\sharp})\otimes m,\phi\otimes\varphi\right\rangle.

Applying
[
25
, Theorem 5.4.2]
, we obtain

\displaystyle\widehat{N}_{t}^{[|\eta|^{2}]}=2\left[\int_{0}^{t}(\alpha|\eta|^{2}+|\overline{\nabla}\eta|^{2})(\widehat{X}_{s})\mathrm{d}s+\widehat{A}_{t}^{\,{\bf Ric}(\eta^{\sharp}\eta^{\sharp})\otimes m}\right]=\widehat{A}_{t}^{\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}+\text{\boldmath$\Delta$}^{2\kappa}\right)|\eta|^{2}}.

Finally, (
10.9
) can be obtained by way of the usual Itô’s formula for semimartingale (
9.10
) with
(
10.8
).
∎

Lemma 10.5
.

Suppose
\alpha>C_{\kappa}
.
Let
\eta=Q_{t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\theta
with
\theta\in D({\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK})\cap L^{\infty}(T^{*}\!M)

and set
\eta_{{\varepsilon}}:=(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}-{\varepsilon}^{p}
with
\varepsilon>0
and
p\in]0,+\infty[
. Then

\mathbb{E}_{(x,a)}[\widehat{M}_{\tau}^{[\eta_{{\varepsilon}}]}]=0
for
\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}
-a.e.
(x,a)\in M\times]0,+\infty[
.

Proof.

It suffices to prove that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{(x,a)}[\langle\widehat{M}^{[\eta_{{\varepsilon}}]}\rangle_{\tau}]=\mathbb{E}_{(x,a)}\left[\int_{0}^{\tau}\left(|\nabla\eta_{{\varepsilon}}|^{2}(\widehat{X}_{s})+\left|\frac{\partial\eta_{{\varepsilon}}}{\partial t}\right|^{2}(\widehat{X}_{s})\right)\mathrm{d}s\right]<\infty

for
\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}
-a.e.
(x,a)
. This clearly holds
in view of the transience of
\widehat{\bf X}_{G}
for
G:=M\times]0,+\infty[
and

|\nabla\eta_{{\varepsilon}}|^{2}+\left|\frac{\partial\eta_{{\varepsilon}}}{\partial t}\right|^{2}\in L^{1}(G;\widehat{\mathfrak{m}})
due to
\eta_{{\varepsilon}}\in D(\widehat{\mathscr{E}})
.
∎

Lemma 10.6
.

Let
\eta=\eta(x,t)=Q_{t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\theta
with
\theta\in D({\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK})
.
Suppose
p\in[1,2]
. Then

\displaystyle\left(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2}\right)^{1-\frac{p}{2}}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}+\Delta_{\ll}\right)\left(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\geq p(\alpha+k)|\eta|^{2}+p(p-1)|\overline{\nabla}\eta|^{2}\quad\mathfrak{m}\text{-a.e.}

Here
k
is the Radon-Nikodym density of
\kappa_{\ll}
with respect to
\mathfrak{m}
:

k:=\frac{\mathrm{d}\kappa_{\ll}}{\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{m}}
.

Proof.

By a simple calculation, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}\left(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}=p(p-2)\left(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}-2}\langle\dot{\eta},\eta\rangle^{2}+\frac{\;p\;}{2}\left(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}-1}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}|\eta|^{2}.

(10.10)

On the other hand, from Corollary
4.22
with
|\eta|^{2}\in D_{f}(\text{\boldmath$\Delta$})_{+}
(Lemma
10.1
), we have

\displaystyle{\text{\boldmath$\Delta$}}(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}=\frac{p}{2}\left(\frac{p}{2}-1\right)\left(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}-2}|\nabla|\eta|^{2}|^{2}\mathfrak{m}+\frac{p}{2}(\widetilde{|\eta|^{2}}+{\varepsilon}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}-1}{\text{\boldmath$\Delta$}}|\eta|^{2}.

(10.11)

Adding (
10.10
) to (
10.11
) and dividing the both sides by
(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}-1}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2}\right)^{1-\frac{p}{2}}

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}+\text{\boldmath$\Delta$}\right)\left(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt=\frac{\;p\;}{2}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}+\text{\boldmath$\Delta$}\right)|\eta|^{2}+\frac{p}{2}\left(\frac{p}{2}-1\right)\left(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2}\right)^{-1}\left(4\langle\dot{\eta},\eta\rangle^{2}+\left|\nabla|\eta|^{2}\right|^{2}\right)\mathfrak{m}

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt=\frac{\;p\;}{2}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}+\text{\boldmath$\Delta$}\right)|\eta|^{2}+\frac{p}{2}\left(\frac{p}{2}-1\right)\left(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2}\right)^{-1}\left(\left|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}|\eta|^{2}\right|^{2}+\left|\nabla|\eta|^{2}\right|^{2}\right)\mathfrak{m}

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt=\frac{\;p\;}{2}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}+\text{\boldmath$\Delta$}\right)|\eta|^{2}+\frac{p}{2}\left(\frac{p}{2}-1\right)\left(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2}\right)^{-1}\left|\overline{\nabla}|\eta|^{2}\right|^{2}\mathfrak{m}

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt=p\left[(\alpha|\eta|^{2}+|\overline{\nabla}\eta|^{2})\mathfrak{m}+{\bf Ric}(\eta^{\sharp},\eta^{\sharp})\right]+\frac{p}{4}(p-2)(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2})^{-1}\left|\overline{\nabla}|\eta|^{2}\right|^{2}\mathfrak{m}.

(10.12)

Hence

\displaystyle\left(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2}\right)^{1-\frac{p}{2}}

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}+\Delta_{\ll}\right)\left(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt=\frac{\;p\;}{2}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}+\Delta_{\ll}\right)|\eta|^{2}+\frac{p}{2}\left(\frac{p}{2}-1\right)\left(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2}\right)^{-1}\left|\overline{\nabla}|\eta|^{2}\right|^{2}\quad\mathfrak{m}\text{-a.e.}

By using Kato’s inequality (Lemma
5.3
),

\displaystyle\left|\nabla|\eta|^{2}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left|2|\eta|\nabla|\eta|\right|^{2}=4|\eta|^{2}\cdot\left|\nabla|\eta|\right|^{2}\leq 4|\eta|^{2}\cdot\left|\nabla\eta^{\sharp}\right|_{\rm HS}^{2}.

(10.13)

Then

\displaystyle\left|\overline{\nabla}|\eta|^{2}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}|\eta|^{2}\right|^{2}+\left|\nabla|\eta|^{2}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip-8.5359pt\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:KatoModified}}}{{\leq}}4\langle\eta,\dot{\eta}\rangle+4|\eta|^{2}\left|\nabla\eta^{\sharp}\right|_{\rm HS}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 4|\eta|^{2}\left(|\dot{\eta}|^{2}+\left|\nabla\eta^{\sharp}\right|_{\rm HS}^{2}\right)=4|\eta|^{2}\left|\overline{\nabla}\eta\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 4(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2})\left|\overline{\nabla}\eta\right|^{2}.

Noting
p-2\leq 0
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2}\right)^{1-\frac{p}{2}}

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}+\Delta_{\ll}\right)\left(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\geq\frac{\;p\;}{2}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}+\Delta_{\ll}\right)|\eta|^{2}+p\left(p-2\right)\left|\overline{\nabla}\eta\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq p\left(\alpha|\eta|^{2}+|\overline{\nabla}\eta|^{2}+{\mathfrak{Ric}}(\eta^{\sharp},\eta^{\sharp})\right)+p\left(p-2\right)\left|\overline{\nabla}\eta\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=p(\alpha|\eta|^{2}+{\mathfrak{Ric}}(\eta^{\sharp},\eta^{\sharp}))+p(p-1)\left|\overline{\nabla}\eta\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69046pt\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:RicciTensor*}}}{{\geq}}p(\alpha+k)|\eta|^{2}+p(p-1)\left|\overline{\nabla}\eta\right|^{2}\quad\mathfrak{m}\text{-a.e.}

∎

Corollary 10.7
.

Let
\eta=\eta(x,t)=Q_{t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\theta
with
\theta\in D({\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK})
.
Suppose
p\in[1,2]
. Then

\displaystyle\left|\overline{\nabla}\eta(x,t)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{p(p-1)}\sup_{s>0}|\eta(x,s)|^{2-p}\varliminf_{{\varepsilon}\to 0}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}+\Delta_{\ll}\right)\left(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}(x,t)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\frac{1}{p-1}\sup_{s>0}|\eta(x,s)|^{2-p}|\eta(x,t)|^{p}(\alpha+k)^{-}(x)\quad\mathfrak{m}\text{-a.e.}~x\in M.

Denote by
\mathscr{P}(M)
, the family of all Borel probability measures on
M
, and
by
\mathscr{B}^{*}(M)
, the family of all universally measurable sets, that is,
\mathscr{B}^{*}(M):=\bigcap_{\nu\in\mathscr{P}(M)}\overline{\mathscr{B}(M)}^{\nu}
, where
\overline{\mathscr{B}(M)}^{\nu}
is the
\nu
-completion of
\mathscr{B}(M)
for
\nu\in\mathscr{P}(M)
.
\mathscr{B}^{*}(M)
also denotes the family of
universally measurable real valued functions. Moreover,
\mathscr{B}_{b}^{*}(M)
(resp.
\mathscr{B}_{+}^{*}(M)
) denotes the family of bounded (resp. non-negative) universally measurable functions.
For
f\in\mathscr{B}_{b}^{*}(M)
, or
f\in\mathscr{B}_{+}^{*}(M)
, we set

\displaystyle q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}f(x):=\mathbb{E}_{x}\left[\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\alpha s-A_{s}^{\kappa}}f(X_{s})\lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}s)\right]

(10.14)

and write
q_{t}^{(\alpha)}f(x)
instead of
q_{t}^{(\alpha),0}f(x)
. Then
q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}f\in\mathscr{B}_{b}^{*}(M)
(resp.
q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}f\in\mathscr{B}_{+}^{*}(M)
) under
f\in\mathscr{B}_{b}^{*}(M)
(resp.
f\in\mathscr{B}_{+}^{*}(M)
).
It is easy to see
q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}1(x)\leq Ce^{-\sqrt{\alpha-C_{\kappa}}t}
(
\alpha\geq C_{\kappa}
) and

q_{t}^{(\alpha)}1(x)\leq e^{-\sqrt{\alpha}t}
(
\alpha\geq 0
).
Take
f\in L^{2}(X;\mathfrak{m})\cap\mathscr{B}_{b}^{*}(M)
or
f\in L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})\cap\mathscr{B}_{+}^{*}(M)
.
According to the equation

\displaystyle(q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}f,g)_{\mathfrak{m}}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\alpha s}(p_{s}^{\kappa}f,g)_{\mathfrak{m}}\lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}s)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\alpha s}(P_{s}^{\kappa}f,g)_{\mathfrak{m}}\lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}s)

(10.15)

\displaystyle=(Q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}f,g)_{\mathfrak{m}}\quad\text{ for any }\quad g\in L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m}),

q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}f
is an
\mathfrak{m}
-version of
Q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}f
.

To prove Theorem
10.9
below, we need the following lemma on the
{\mathscr{E}}
-quasi-continuity of
x\mapsto q_{t}^{\alpha,\kappa}(x)
:

Lemma 10.8
.

For
u\in L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})\cap\mathscr{B}^{*}(M)
,
t>0
and
\alpha>C_{\kappa}
, we set

\displaystyle Q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}u(x):

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\alpha s}P_{s}^{\kappa}u(x)\lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}s),

(10.16)

\displaystyle q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}u(x):

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\alpha s}p_{s}^{\kappa}u(x)\lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}s).

(10.17)

Then
Q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}u\in D(\mathscr{E})
and

q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}u
is an

{\mathscr{E}}
-quasi-continuous
\mathfrak{m}
-version of
Q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}u
.

Proof.

The first assertion is clear from the following estimate: for
\alpha_{0}:=\alpha\|U_{\alpha}\kappa^{-}\|_{\infty}
in (
2.4
),

\displaystyle\|Q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}u\|_{L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\alpha s}\|P_{s}^{\kappa}u\|_{L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})}\lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}s)

\displaystyle\leq C(\kappa)e^{-\sqrt{\alpha-C_{\kappa}}t}\|u\|_{L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})}<\infty

(10.18)

and

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{\alpha_{0}}^{\kappa}(Q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}u,Q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}u)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\alpha s}\mathscr{E}_{\alpha_{0}}^{\kappa}(P_{s}^{\kappa}u,P_{s}^{\kappa}u)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}s)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\alpha s}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2s}}\lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}s)\right)\|u\|_{L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})}<\infty.

(10.19)

Fix
t>0
. Suppose
u\in L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})\cap C_{b}(M)
.
Note that
p_{s}^{\kappa}u
is an
\mathscr{E}
-quasi continuous
\mathfrak{m}
-version of

P_{s}^{\kappa}u
by
[
37
, Theorem 7.3]
.
Then it is easy to see that for the following
D(\mathscr{E})
-valued step function
s\mapsto p_{s}^{n,\kappa}u
,
p_{s}^{n,\kappa}u

is
\mathscr{E}
-quasi continuous and
\{p_{s}^{n,\kappa}u\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}

\mathscr{E}_{1}
-converges to
P_{s}^{\kappa}u
for each
s>0
:

\displaystyle p_{s}^{n,\kappa}u(x):=\sum_{k=0}^{n2^{n}-1}p_{\frac{k+1}{2^{n}}}^{\kappa}u(x){\bf 1}_{[\frac{k}{2^{n}},\frac{k+1}{2^{n}}[}(s)+p_{n}^{\kappa}u(x){\bf 1}_{[n,+\infty[}(s).

We can see

\displaystyle\|p_{s}^{n,\kappa}u\|_{L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{n2^{n}-1}\|p_{\frac{k+1}{2^{n}}}^{\kappa}u\|_{L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})}{\bf 1}_{[\frac{k}{2^{n}},\frac{k+1}{2^{n}}[}(s)+\|p_{n}^{\kappa}u\|_{L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})}{\bf 1}_{[n,+\infty[}(s)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{n2^{n}-1}\|P_{\frac{k+1}{2^{n}}}^{\kappa}u\|_{L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})}{\bf 1}_{[\frac{k}{2^{n}},\frac{k+1}{2^{n}}[}(s)+\|P_{n}^{\kappa}u\|_{L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})}{\bf 1}_{[n,+\infty[}(s)

\displaystyle\leq C(\kappa)e^{C_{\kappa}\left(s+\frac{1}{2^{n}}\right)}\|u\|_{L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})}\leq C(\kappa)e^{C_{\kappa}\left(s+1\right)}\|u\|_{L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})}.

(10.20)

Then,

\displaystyle q_{t}^{(\alpha),n,\kappa}u(x):

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\alpha s}p_{s}^{n,\kappa}u(x)\lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}s)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{n2^{n}-1}p_{\frac{k+1}{2^{n}}}^{\kappa}u(x)\int_{\frac{k}{2^{n}}}^{\frac{k+1}{2^{n}}}e^{-\alpha s}\lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}s)+p_{n}^{\kappa}u(x)\int_{n}^{\infty}e^{-\alpha s}\lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}s)

satisfies
q_{t}^{(\alpha),n,\kappa}\in D(\mathscr{E})
and it

\mathscr{E}_{1}
-converges to

Q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}u
. Indeed, for
\alpha_{0}:=\alpha\|U_{\alpha}\kappa^{-}\|_{\infty}
and
C>0

specified in (
2.4
),

\displaystyle\|q_{t}^{(\alpha),n,\kappa}u-Q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}u\|_{L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\alpha s}\|p_{s}^{n,\kappa}u-P_{s}^{\kappa}u\|_{L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})}\lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}s)

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt\to 0\quad\text{ as }\quad n\to\infty

(10.21)

and

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{\alpha_{0}}^{\kappa}

\displaystyle(q_{t}^{(\alpha),n,\kappa}u-Q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}u,q_{t}^{(\alpha),n,\kappa}u-Q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}u)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\alpha s}\mathscr{E}_{\alpha_{0}}^{\kappa}(p_{s}^{n,\kappa}u-P_{s}^{\kappa}u,p_{s}^{n,\kappa}u-P_{s}^{\kappa}u)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}s)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{C}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\alpha s}\mathscr{E}_{1}(p_{s}^{n,\kappa}u-P_{s}^{\kappa}u,p_{s}^{n,\kappa}u-P_{s}^{\kappa}u)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}s)

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt\to 0\quad\text{ as }\quad n\to\infty,

(10.22)

where we use the Lebesgue’s dominated convergence theorem
under the following observation: by (
10.20
)

\displaystyle\|p_{s}^{n,\kappa}u-P_{s}^{\kappa}u\|_{L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})}

\displaystyle\leq 2C(\kappa)e^{C_{\kappa}(s+1)},

and
for any
s>0
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
s\geq n
or
s\in[\frac{k}{2^{n}},\frac{k+1}{2^{n}}[
for some
k\in\{0,\cdots,n2^{n}-1\}
.
If
s\geq n
, then by (
2.4
)

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}(p_{s}^{n,\kappa}u-P_{s}^{\kappa}u,p_{s}^{n,\kappa}u-P_{s}^{\kappa}u)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq(1-\|U_{\alpha}\kappa^{-}\|_{\infty})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathscr{E}_{\alpha_{0}}^{\kappa}(p_{n}^{\kappa}u-P_{s}^{\kappa}u,p_{n}^{n,\kappa}u-P_{s}^{\kappa}u)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq(1-\|U_{\alpha}\kappa^{-}\|_{\infty})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2n}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2s}}\right)\|u\|_{L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})}

\displaystyle\leq(1-\|U_{\alpha}\kappa^{-}\|_{\infty})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{s}}\|u\|_{L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})}.

If
s\in[\frac{k}{2^{n}},\frac{k+1}{2^{n}}[
for some
k\in\{0,\cdots,n2^{n}-1\}
, then by (
2.4
)

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}(p_{s}^{n,\kappa}u-P_{s}^{\kappa}u,p_{s}^{n,\kappa}u-P_{s}^{\kappa}u)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq(1-\|U_{\alpha}\kappa^{-}\|_{\infty})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathscr{E}_{\alpha_{0}}^{\kappa}(p_{\frac{k+1}{2^{n}}}^{\kappa}u-P_{s}^{\kappa}u,p_{\frac{k+1}{2^{n}}}^{n,\kappa}u-P_{s}^{\kappa}u)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq(1-\|U_{\alpha}\kappa^{-}\|_{\infty})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2(k+1)/2^{n}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2s}}\right)\|u\|_{L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})}

\displaystyle\leq(1-\|U_{\alpha}\kappa^{-}\|_{\infty})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{s}}\|u\|_{L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})}.

Thus we can justify (
10.21
) and (
10.22
). Since
u\in C_{b}(M)
and
2\kappa^{-}\in S_{E\!K}({\bf X})
,

s\mapsto p_{s}^{\kappa}u(x)
is continuous on
[0,+\infty[
, consequently,
p_{s}^{n,\kappa}u(x)
converges to
p_{s}^{\kappa}u(x)
as
n\to\infty
for each
x\in M
. This means that

q_{t}^{(\alpha),n,\kappa}u(x)=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\alpha s}p_{s}^{n,\kappa}(x)\lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}s)
converges to
q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}u(x)=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\alpha s}p_{s}^{\kappa}u(x)(x)\lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}s)
as
n\to\infty
for each
x\in M
.
Therefore,
q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}u
is an
\mathscr{E}
-quasi continuous
\mathfrak{m}
-version of
Q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}u
by
[
25
, Theorem 2.1.4(ii)]
.

Next we prove the assertion for general
u\in L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})\cap\mathscr{B}(M)
.
Let us put
H:=\{u\in L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})\cap\mathscr{B}_{+}(M)\mid q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}u\text{ is an }\mathscr{E}\text{-quasi continuous }\mathfrak{m}\text{-version of }Q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}u\}
. We have just proved

H\supset L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})\cap C_{b}(M)_{+}
, and
H
satisfies (H.3) of
[
25
, Lemma 4.2.3]
.
Indeed, for any open set
G
, we choose an increasing sequence of
\mathfrak{m}
-finite open sets
\{G_{n}\}
.
Since
(M,\tau)
can be metrizable in view of the quasi-regularity of
(\mathscr{E},D(\mathscr{E})

(in fact,
M
can be replaced with a countable union of compact
\mathscr{E}
-nest),
u_{n}:=n{\sf d}(x,G_{n}^{c})\land 1\uparrow{\bf 1}_{G}
.
(H.1) of
[
25
, Lemma 4.2.3]
is trivially satisfied. To check (H.2) of
[
25
, Lemma 4.2.3]
, let
u_{n}\in H
increase to
u\in L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})
. Then
Q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}u_{n}

\mathscr{E}_{1}
-converges to

Q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}u
. Indeed, for
\alpha_{0}:=C_{\kappa}
, we see

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{\alpha_{0}}^{\kappa}(Q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}u_{n}-Q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}u,

\displaystyle Q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}u_{n}-Q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}u)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\alpha s}\mathscr{E}_{\alpha_{0}}^{\kappa}(P_{s}^{\kappa}(u_{n}-u),P_{s}^{\kappa}(u_{n}-u))^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}s)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\alpha s}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2s}}\|u_{n}-u\|_{L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})}\lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}s)

\displaystyle=\|u_{n}-u\|_{L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\alpha s}\frac{\lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}s)}{\sqrt{2s}}

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt\to 0\quad\text{ as }\quad n\to\infty

and

\displaystyle\|Q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}u_{n}-Q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}u\|_{L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\alpha s}\|P_{s}^{\kappa}(u_{n}-u)\|_{L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})}\lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}s)

\displaystyle\leq C(\kappa)e^{-\sqrt{\alpha-C_{\kappa}}t}\|u_{n}-u\|_{L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})}\to 0\quad\text{ as }\quad n\to\infty.

Since
q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}u_{n}
is an
\mathscr{E}
-quasi continuous
\mathfrak{m}
-version of
Q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}u_{n}
and converges to
q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}u
pointwise, we get
u\in H
by
[
25
, Theorem 2.1.4(ii)]
.
Then
[
25
, Lemma 4.2.3]
implies that the assertion holds for
u\in L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})\cap\mathscr{B}(M)
.
The extension to the universally measurable
u
is clear.
∎

Theorem 10.9
.

Suppose
p\in]1,2]
,
\kappa^{-}\in S_{K}({\bf X})
and
\alpha>C_{\kappa}
. Then
the
G
-function

G_{\theta}
can be extended to be in
L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)

for
\theta\in L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)
and the following inequality holds for

\theta\in L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)

\displaystyle\|G_{\theta}\|_{L^{p}(M;\mathfrak{m})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\theta\|_{L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)}.

(10.23)

Proof.

First we assume

\theta\in D({\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK})\cap L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)
. By setting
\eta(x,t):=Q_{t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\theta(x)
, we see from Corollary
10.7

\displaystyle G_{\theta}(x)^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}t\left|\overline{\nabla}\eta(x,t)\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p(p-1)}\sup_{s>0}|\eta(x,s)|^{2-p}\int_{0}^{\infty}t\varliminf_{{\varepsilon}\to 0}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}+\Delta_{\ll}\right)\left(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}(x,t)\mathrm{d}t,

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\frac{1}{p-1}\sup_{s>0}|\eta(x,s)|^{2-p}(\alpha+k)^{-}(x)\int_{0}^{\infty}t|\eta(x,t)|^{p}\mathrm{d}t.

Using the Hölder inequality with exponent
\frac{2}{2-p}
and
\frac{2}{p}
, we have

\displaystyle\|G_{\theta}\|_{L^{p}(M;\mathfrak{m})}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{p(p-1)}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\int_{M}\sup_{s>0}|\eta(x,s)|^{\frac{p(2-p)}{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\times\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}t\left[\varliminf_{{\varepsilon}\to 0}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}+\Delta_{\ll}\right)(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}+p|\eta|^{p}(\alpha+k)^{-}\right]\mathrm{d}t\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{m}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{p(p-1)}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\|\sup_{s>0}|\eta(\cdot,s)|\|_{L^{p}(M;\mathfrak{m})}^{\frac{p(2-p)}{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\times\left(\int_{M}\int_{0}^{\infty}t\left[\varliminf_{{\varepsilon}\to 0}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}+\Delta_{\ll}\right)(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}+p|\eta|^{p}(\alpha+k)^{-}\right]\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{m}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}.

From Proposition
8.2
, we have

\displaystyle\|G_{\theta}\|_{L^{p}(M;\mathfrak{m})}^{p}\leq C_{p}\|\theta\|_{L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)}^{\frac{p(2-p)}{2}}\left(\int_{M}\int_{0}^{\infty}t\left[\varliminf_{{\varepsilon}\to 0}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}+\Delta_{\ll}\right)(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}+p|\eta|^{p}(\alpha+k)^{-}\right]\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{m}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}.

Then, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\int_{M}\int_{0}^{\infty}t\left[\varliminf_{{\varepsilon}\to 0}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}+\Delta_{\ll}\right)(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}+p|\eta|^{p}(\alpha+k)^{-}\right]\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{m}\lesssim\|\theta\|_{L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)}^{p}.

(10.24)

The left-hand side of (
10.24
) is estimated as follows:

\displaystyle\int_{M}\int_{0}^{\infty}

\displaystyle t\left[\varliminf_{{\varepsilon}\to 0}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}+\Delta_{\ll}\right)(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}+p|\eta|^{p}(\alpha+k)^{-}\right]\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{m}

\displaystyle\leq\varliminf_{{\varepsilon}\to 0}\lim_{a\to\infty}\int_{M}\int_{0}^{\infty}(t\land a)\left[\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}+\Delta_{\ll}\right)(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}+p|\eta|^{p}(\alpha+k)^{-}\right]\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{m}

\displaystyle=\varliminf_{{\varepsilon}\to 0}\lim_{a\to\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{m}\otimes\delta_{a}}\left[\int_{0}^{\tau}\left[\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}+\Delta_{\ll}\right)(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}(\widehat{X}_{s})+p|\eta|^{p}(\alpha+k)^{-}(\widehat{X}_{s})\right]\mathrm{d}s\right].

Here we use Proposition
9.4
(
[
49
, Proposition 6.1]
).
On the other hand, the signed finite smooth measure

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}+\Delta_{\ll}\right)(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}\mathfrak{m}+p|\eta|^{p}(\alpha+k)^{-}\mathfrak{m}

is estimated above by

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}+\text{\boldmath$\Delta$}\right)(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}+p\widetilde{|\eta|^{p}}\kappa^{-}.

Indeed,

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}+\Delta_{\ll}\right)

\displaystyle(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}\mathfrak{m}+p|\eta|^{p}(\alpha+k)^{-}\mathfrak{m}

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt=\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}+\text{\boldmath$\Delta$}\right)(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}-\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}+\text{\boldmath$\Delta$}\right)_{\perp}(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}+p|\eta|^{p}(\alpha+k)^{-}\mathfrak{m}

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt=\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}+\text{\boldmath$\Delta$}\right)(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}-p(\widetilde{|\eta|^{2}}+{\varepsilon}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}-1}{\bf Ric}_{\perp}(\eta^{\sharp},\eta^{\sharp})+p|\eta|^{p}(\alpha+k)^{-}\mathfrak{m}

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt=\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}+\text{\boldmath$\Delta$}\right)(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}-p(\widetilde{|\eta|^{2}}+{\varepsilon}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}-1}{\bf Ric}_{\perp}^{\kappa}(\eta^{\sharp},\eta^{\sharp})

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+p(\widetilde{|\eta|^{2}}+{\varepsilon}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}-1}\widetilde{|\eta|^{2}}\kappa_{\perp}+p|\eta|^{p}\kappa_{\ll}^{-}

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\leq\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}+\text{\boldmath$\Delta$}\right)(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}+p(\widetilde{|\eta|^{2}}+{\varepsilon}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}-1}\widetilde{|\eta|^{2}}\kappa^{-}_{\perp}+p|\eta|^{p}\kappa_{\ll}^{-}
