\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\leq\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}+\text{\boldmath$\Delta$}\right)(|\eta|^{2}+{\varepsilon}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}+p\widetilde{|\eta|^{p}}\kappa^{-}.

Here we use
p\leq 2
,

{\bf Ric}_{\perp}^{\kappa}(\eta^{\sharp},\eta^{\sharp})\geq 0
and
{\bf Ric}_{\perp}(\eta^{\sharp},\eta^{\sharp})={\bf Ric}^{\kappa}_{\perp}(\eta^{\sharp},\eta^{\sharp})+\widetilde{|\eta|^{2}}\kappa_{\perp}
. Then the left-hand side of (
10.24
) is estimated above by

\displaystyle\varliminf_{{\varepsilon}\to 0}

\displaystyle\lim_{a\to\infty}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{m}\otimes\delta_{a}}\left[\widehat{A}_{\tau}^{\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}+\text{\boldmath$\Delta$}\right)\eta_{{\varepsilon}}}\right]+p\,\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{m}\otimes\delta_{a}}\left[\int_{0}^{\tau}\widetilde{|\eta|^{p}}(\widehat{X}_{s})\mathrm{d}\widehat{A}_{s}^{\,\widehat{\kappa}^{-}}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=\varliminf_{{\varepsilon}\to 0}\lim_{a\to\infty}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{m}\otimes\delta_{a}}\left[\widehat{N}_{\tau}^{[\eta_{{\varepsilon}}]}\right]+p\,\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{m}\otimes\delta_{a}}\left[\int_{0}^{\tau}\widetilde{|\eta|^{p}}(\widehat{X}_{s})\mathrm{d}\widehat{A}_{s}^{\,\widehat{\kappa}^{-}}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=\varliminf_{{\varepsilon}\to 0}\lim_{a\to\infty}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{m}\otimes\delta_{a}}\left[\widetilde{{\eta}_{{\varepsilon}}}(\widehat{X}_{\tau})-\widetilde{{\eta}_{{\varepsilon}}}(\widehat{X}_{0})\right]+p\,\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{m}\otimes\delta_{a}}\left[\int_{0}^{\tau}\widetilde{|\eta|^{p}}(\widehat{X}_{s})\mathrm{d}\widehat{A}_{s}^{\,\widehat{\kappa}^{-}}\right]\right)\quad(\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:QuadraticVariation}})

\displaystyle\leq\varliminf_{{\varepsilon}\to 0}\lim_{a\to\infty}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{m}\otimes\delta_{a}}\left[(\widetilde{|\eta|^{2}}(\widehat{X}_{\tau})+{\varepsilon}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}-{\varepsilon}^{p}\right]+p\,\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{m}\otimes\delta_{a}}\left[\int_{0}^{\tau}\widetilde{|\eta|^{p}}(\widehat{X}_{s})\mathrm{d}\widehat{A}_{s}^{\,\widehat{\kappa}^{-}}\right]\right)\quad(\widetilde{\eta_{{\varepsilon}}}(\widehat{X}_{0})\geq 0)

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{a\to\infty}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{m}\otimes\delta_{a}}\left[\widetilde{|\eta|^{p}}(\widehat{X}_{\tau})\right]+p\,\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{m}\otimes\delta_{a}}\left[\int_{0}^{\tau}\widetilde{|\eta|^{p}}(\widehat{X}_{s})\mathrm{d}\widehat{A}_{s}^{\,\widehat{\kappa}^{-}}\right]\right)\quad((a^{2}+b^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}-b^{p}\leq a^{p})

\displaystyle\leq\|\theta\|_{L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)}^{p}+\lim_{a\to\infty}p\,\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{m}\otimes\delta_{a}}\left[\int_{0}^{\tau}\widetilde{|\eta|^{p}}(\widehat{X}_{s})\mathrm{d}\widehat{A}_{s}^{\,\widehat{\kappa}^{-}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69046pt\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:ShigekawaProp3.10}}}{{=}}\|\theta\|_{L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)}^{p}+\lim_{a\to\infty}p\int_{M}\int_{0}^{\infty}(a\land t)\widetilde{|\eta|^{p}}(x,t)\mathrm{d}t\kappa^{-}(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle=\|\theta\|_{L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)}^{p}+p\int_{M}\int_{0}^{\infty}t\widetilde{|\eta|^{p}}(x,t)\mathrm{d}t\kappa^{-}(\mathrm{d}x).

Here we use
\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{m}\otimes\delta_{a}}[\widetilde{|\eta|^{2}}(\widehat{X}_{\tau})]=\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{m}\otimes\delta_{a}}[\widetilde{|\eta|^{2}}({X}_{\tau},0)]=\int_{M}\widetilde{|\eta|^{p}}(x,0)\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{m}=\int_{M}|\theta|^{p}\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{m}
in the last inequality, and
implicitly use a discrete limit for
a\to\infty
avoiding Lebesgue measure zero set appeared in Lemma
10.5
.
We can not use Lenglart-Lepingle-Pratelli inequality, because
\eta_{{\varepsilon}}(\widehat{X}_{t})
may not be a submartingale.
Since the Bochner integral
\eta(x,t)=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\alpha s}P_{s}^{\rm HK}\theta(x)\lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}s)
is an

L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)
-limit of

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{n2^{n}-1}P_{\frac{k}{2^{n}}}^{\rm HK}\theta(x)\int_{\frac{k}{2^{n}}}^{\frac{k+1}{2^{n}}}e^{-\alpha s}\lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}s)+P_{n}^{\rm HK}\theta(x)\int_{n}^{\infty}e^{-\alpha s}\lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}s),

its pointwise norm converges to
|\eta(x,t)|
in
\mathfrak{m}
-measure with respect to
x\in M
as
n\to\infty
. Thus we see

\displaystyle|\eta(x,t)|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\alpha s}P_{s}^{\rm HK}\theta(x)\lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}s)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\alpha s}|P_{s}^{\rm HK}\theta(x)|\lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}s)\quad\mathfrak{m}\text{-a.e.}

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69046pt\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:HSU}}}{{\leq}}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\alpha s}P_{s}^{\kappa}|\theta|(x)\lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}s)=Q_{t}^{\alpha,\kappa}|\theta|(x).

From this,
we have
\widetilde{|\eta|}(x,t)\leq q_{t}^{\alpha}|\theta|(x)

\mathscr{E}
-q.e.
x\in M
for a.e.
t>0
by Lemma
10.8
,.
Note here that for a.e.
t>0
the restriction
\widetilde{|\eta|}(\cdot,t)

of
\widehat{\mathscr{E}}
-quasi continuous function

\widetilde{|\eta|}
is
\mathscr{E}
-quasi continuous by Lemma
9.1
. Moreover, we take
|\theta|
as a Borel
\mathfrak{m}
-version.
Then the second term of the right hand side above is

\displaystyle p\int_{M}\int_{0}^{\infty}t\,\widetilde{|\eta|^{p}}(x,t)\mathrm{d}t\,\kappa^{-}(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\leq p\int_{M}\int_{0}^{\infty}t\,q_{t}^{\alpha}|\theta|(x)^{p}\mathrm{d}t\,\kappa^{-}(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\leq p\int_{M}\int_{0}^{\infty}t\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\alpha ps}p_{s}^{\kappa}|\theta|(x)^{p}\lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}s)\mathrm{d}t\,\kappa^{-}(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle=p\int_{M}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left\{\int_{0}^{\infty}t\left(\frac{t}{2\sqrt{\pi}}e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{4s}}s^{-\frac{3}{2}}\right)\mathrm{d}t\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\times\left(\mathbb{E}_{x}\left[e^{-\alpha s-A_{s}^{\kappa}}|\theta|(X_{s})\right]\right)^{p}\mathrm{d}s\,\kappa^{-}(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69046pt\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:Expectation}}}{{\leq}}p\int_{M}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}_{x}\left[e^{-p(\alpha s+A_{s}^{\kappa})}|\theta|^{p}(X_{s})\right]\mathrm{d}s\,\kappa^{-}(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\leq p\int_{M}\mathbb{E}_{x}\left[\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-p\alpha s+pA_{s}^{\kappa^{-}}}|\theta|^{p}(X_{s})\mathrm{d}s\right]\kappa^{-}(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle=p\int_{M}|\theta|^{p}(x)\mathbb{E}_{x}\left[\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-p\alpha s+pA_{s}^{\kappa^{-}}}\mathrm{d}A_{s}^{\kappa^{-}}\right]\mathfrak{m}(\mathrm{d}x),

where
we use the generalization of Revuz correspondence (see
[
2
, (3.11)]
) and

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}t\left(\frac{t}{2\sqrt{\pi}}e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{4s}}s^{-\frac{3}{2}}\right)\mathrm{d}t=1.

(10.25)

By using the probability law
\mathbb{P}_{x}^{(p\alpha)}
for the killed process
{\bf X}^{(p\alpha)}
characterized by
\mathbb{E}_{x}^{(p\alpha)}[f(X_{t})]=\mathbb{E}_{x}[e^{-p\alpha t}f(X_{t})]
,
f\in\mathscr{B}_{+}(M)
, we have

\displaystyle p\,\mathbb{E}_{x}\left[\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-p\alpha s+pA_{s}^{\kappa^{-}}}\mathrm{d}A_{s}^{\kappa^{-}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{x}\left[\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-p\alpha s}\mathrm{d}e^{pA_{s}^{\kappa^{-}}}\right]=\mathbb{E}_{x}^{(p\alpha)}\left[e^{pA_{\infty}^{\kappa^{-}}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{1-p\left\|\mathbb{E}_{\cdot}^{(p\alpha)}\left[A_{\infty}^{\kappa^{-}}\right]\right\|_{L^{\infty}(M;\mathfrak{m})}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{1-p\left\|\mathbb{E}_{\cdot}\left[\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-p\alpha s}\mathrm{d}A_{s}^{\kappa^{-}}\right]\right\|_{L^{\infty}(M;\mathfrak{m})}}<\infty,\quad\mathfrak{m}\text{-a.e.~}x\in M.

Then, we can conclude that the left-hand side of (
10.24
) is estimated by

\displaystyle\left(1+\frac{1}{1-p\left\|\mathbb{E}_{\cdot}\left[\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-p\alpha s}\mathrm{d}A_{s}^{\kappa^{-}}\right]\right\|_{L^{\infty}(M;\mathfrak{m})}}\right)\|\theta\|_{L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)}^{p}.

Note here that under
\alpha>C_{\kappa}
, we have
p\alpha>C_{p\kappa}
, hence from Lemma
2.1

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}_{\cdot}\left[\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-p\alpha s}\mathrm{d}A_{s}^{\kappa^{-}}\right]\right\|_{L^{\infty}(M;\mathfrak{m})}<1/p.

Any
\theta\in L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)\cap L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)
can be
approximated by a sequence
\{P_{1/n}^{\rm HK}\theta\}

of
D({\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK})\cap L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)
in
L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)
-norm and in
L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)
-norm by
Corollary
6.9
.
Then one can conclude the statement of
Theorem
10.9
holds
for general
\theta\in L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)\cap L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)
in view of the triangle inequality

|G_{\theta_{1}}-G_{\theta_{2}}|\leq G_{\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}}
.
Moreover, since any
\theta\in L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)
can be approximated by a sequence of
L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)\cap L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)
in
L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)
, we can conclude the assertion.
∎

Now we define the
H
-function by

\displaystyle H_{\theta}^{\rightarrow}(x):

\displaystyle=\left\{\int_{0}^{\infty}tQ_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}\left(g_{\theta}^{\rightarrow}(\cdot,t)^{2}\right)(x)\mathrm{d}t\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle H_{\theta}^{\uparrow}(x):

\displaystyle=\left\{\int_{0}^{\infty}tQ_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}\left(g_{\theta}^{\uparrow}(\cdot,t)^{2}\right)(x)\mathrm{d}t\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle H_{\theta}(x):

\displaystyle=\left\{\int_{0}^{\infty}tQ_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}\left(g_{\theta}(\cdot,t)^{2}\right)(x)\mathrm{d}t\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Lemma 10.10
.

We have
G_{\theta}^{\rightarrow}(x)\leq 2\sqrt{C(\kappa)}H_{\theta}^{\rightarrow}(x)
.

Proof.

We calculate

\displaystyle g_{\theta}^{\rightarrow}(x,2t)^{2}

\displaystyle=\left|\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial s}Q_{s}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\theta(x)\right|_{s=2t}\right|^{2}=\left|\sqrt{\alpha-{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK}}Q_{2t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\theta(x)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left|Q_{t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\sqrt{\alpha-{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK}}Q_{t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\theta(x)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(Q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}\left|\sqrt{\alpha-{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK}}Q_{t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\theta\right|(x)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq Q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}1\cdot Q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}\left|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}Q_{t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\theta\right|^{2}(x).

Then

\displaystyle G_{\theta}^{\rightarrow}(x)

\displaystyle=\left\{\int_{0}^{\infty}tg_{\theta}^{\rightarrow}(x,t)^{2}\mathrm{d}t\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}}=\left\{4\int_{0}^{\infty}tg_{\theta}^{\rightarrow}(x,2t)^{2}\mathrm{d}t\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\{\int_{0}^{\infty}tQ_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}1\cdot Q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}g_{\theta}^{\rightarrow}(\cdot,t)^{2}(x)\mathrm{d}t\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{C(\kappa)}\left\{\int_{0}^{\infty}tQ_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}g_{\theta}^{\rightarrow}(\cdot,t)^{2}(x)\mathrm{d}t\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=2\sqrt{C(\kappa)}H_{\theta}^{\rightarrow}(x).

∎

Theorem 10.11
.

Suppose
p\in]2,+\infty[
,
\kappa^{-}_{\ll}=-R\mathfrak{m}
with
R\geq 0
,
\kappa_{\perp}^{-}=0
and
\alpha>C_{\kappa}
. Then
the
G
-function

G_{\theta}^{\rightarrow}
can be extended to be in
L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)

for
\theta\in L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)
and the following inequality holds for

\theta\in L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)

\displaystyle\|G_{\theta}^{\rightarrow}\|_{L^{p}(M;\mathfrak{m})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\theta\|_{L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)}.

(10.26)

Proof.

By Lemma
10.10
, it suffices to prove
\|H_{\theta}\|_{L^{p}(M;\mathfrak{m})}\lesssim\|\theta\|_{L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)}
.

\displaystyle\|H_{\theta}\|_{L^{p}(M;\mathfrak{m})}

\displaystyle=\left\|\left\{\int_{0}^{\infty}tQ_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}\left(g_{\theta}(\cdot,t)^{2}\right)\mathrm{d}t\right\}^{\frac{p}{2}}\right\|_{L^{1}(M;\mathfrak{m})}

\displaystyle=\int_{M}\left\{\int_{0}^{\infty}tQ_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}\left(g_{\theta}(\cdot,t)^{2}\right)\mathrm{d}t\right\}^{\frac{p}{2}}\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{m}

(10.27)

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{M}\left\{\int_{0}^{\infty}(t\land a_{n})Q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}\left(g_{\theta}(\cdot,t)^{2}\right)\mathrm{d}t\right\}^{\frac{p}{2}}\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{m}

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{m}\otimes\delta_{a_{n}}}\left[\left\{\int_{0}^{\infty}(t\land a_{n})Q_{t}^{(\alpha),\kappa}\left(g_{\theta}(\cdot,t)^{2}(X_{\tau})\right)\mathrm{d}t\right\}^{\frac{p}{2}}\right].

By using
[
23
, (3.34)]
, the right hand side of (
10.27
) is estimated above by

\displaystyle\varliminf_{n\to\infty}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{m}\otimes\delta_{a_{n}}}\left[\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{m}\otimes\delta_{a_{n}}}\left[\left.\int_{0}^{\tau}g_{\theta}^{2}(\widehat{X}_{s})\mathrm{d}s\,\right|\,X_{\tau}\right]^{\frac{p}{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=\varliminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{m}\otimes\delta_{a_{n}}}\left[\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{m}\otimes\delta_{a_{n}}}\left[\left.\left(\int_{0}^{\tau}g_{\theta}^{2}(\widehat{X}_{s})\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\right|\,X_{\tau}\right]\right]

\displaystyle=\varliminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{m}\otimes\delta_{a_{n}}}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{\tau}g_{\theta}^{2}(\widehat{X}_{s})\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\right].

On the other hand, under the conditions
\kappa^{-}_{\ll}=-R\mathfrak{m}
with
R\geq 0
and
\kappa^{-}_{\perp}=0
, the semimartingale
(
9.10
) is actually a submartingale, because

\displaystyle\widehat{A}_{t}^{\,\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial a^{2}}+\text{\boldmath$\Delta$}\right)|\eta|^{2}}

\displaystyle=N_{t}^{|\eta|^{2}}=2\int_{0}^{t}(\alpha|\eta|^{2}+|\overline{\nabla}\eta|^{2})(\widehat{X}_{s})\mathrm{d}s+2\widehat{A}_{t}^{\,{\bf\bf Ric}(\eta^{\sharp},\eta^{\sharp})\otimes m}

\displaystyle\geq 2\int_{0}^{t}((\alpha-R)|\eta|^{2}+|\overline{\nabla}\eta|^{2})(\widehat{X}_{s})\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\geq 2\int_{0}^{t}|\overline{\nabla}\eta|^{2}(\widehat{X}_{s})\mathrm{d}s=2\int_{0}^{t}g_{\theta}^{2}(\widehat{X}_{s})\mathrm{d}s

under
\alpha>C_{\kappa}=R
. Then one can apply Lenglart-Lepingle-Pratelli inequality (see
[
23
, (3.39)]
) and Doob’s inequality so that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{m}\otimes\delta_{a_{n}}}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{\tau}g_{\theta}^{2}(\widehat{X}_{s})\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2^{-\frac{p}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{m}\otimes\delta_{a_{n}}}\left[\left(\widehat{A}_{t}^{\,\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial a^{2}}+\text{\boldmath$\Delta$}\right)|\eta|^{2}}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{p}{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{m}\otimes\delta_{a_{n}}}\left[\left||\eta|^{2}(\widehat{X}_{\tau})-|\eta|^{2}(\widehat{X}_{0})\right|^{\frac{p}{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{p}{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{m}\otimes\delta_{a_{n}}}\left[\left||Q_{0}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}(X_{\tau})|^{2}-|Q_{a_{n}}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}(X_{0})|^{2}\right|^{\frac{p}{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{p}{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{m}\otimes\delta_{a_{n}}}\left[|Q_{0}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}(X_{\tau})|^{p}+|Q_{a_{n}}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}(X_{0})|^{p}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{p}{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\left(\|\theta\|_{L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)}+\|Q_{a_{n}}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\theta\|_{L^{p}(M;\mathfrak{m})}^{p}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{p}{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\left(\|\theta\|_{L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)}+Ce^{-\sqrt{\alpha-C_{\kappa}}a_{n}}\|\theta\|_{L^{p}(M;\mathfrak{m})}^{p}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\theta\|_{L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)}.

∎

11

Proof of Theorem
1.2

For
f\in L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})\cap L^{p}(M;\mathfrak{m})
and
\alpha>0
, we introduce the
G
-functions for
f
as follows:

\displaystyle{g_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rightarrow}}{{f}}}}(x,t)

\displaystyle:=\left|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}(Q_{t}^{(\alpha)}f)(x)\right|,\qquad\quad\quad{G_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rightarrow}}{{f}}}}(x):=\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}t{g_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rightarrow}}{{f}}}}(x,t)^{2}\mathrm{d}t\right)^{1/2},

\displaystyle{g_{f}^{\uparrow}}(x,t)

\displaystyle:=\left|\mathrm{d}(Q_{t}^{(\alpha)}f)(x)\right|,\qquad\qquad\quad{G_{f}^{\uparrow}}(x):=\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}t{g_{f}^{\uparrow}}(x,t)^{2}\mathrm{d}t\right)^{1/2},

\displaystyle g_{f}(x,t)

\displaystyle:=\sqrt{{g_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rightarrow}}{{f}}}}(x,t)^{2}+{g_{f}^{\uparrow}}(x,t)^{2}},\qquad\quad G_{f}(x):=\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}tg_{f}(x,t)^{2}\mathrm{d}t\right)^{1/2}.

It is proved in
[
23
, Theorem 1.2]
that
G
-functions
g_{f}^{\rightarrow}(\cdot,t)
,
g_{f}^{\uparrow}(\cdot,t)
,
g_{f}(\cdot,t)
,

G_{f}^{\rightarrow}
,
G_{f}^{\uparrow}
and
G_{f}
can be extended for
f\in L^{p}(M;\mathfrak{m})
and

\|G_{f}\|_{L^{p}(M;\mathfrak{m})}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{p}(M;\mathfrak{m})}
, in particular,
\|G_{f}^{\uparrow}\|_{L^{p}(M;\mathfrak{m})}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{p}(M;\mathfrak{m})}
.

Lemma 11.1
.

Take
p\in]1,+\infty[
and its conjugate exponent
q:=p/(p-1)\in]1,+\infty[
. For
f\in L^{p}(M;\mathfrak{m})
, we define the
Riesz transform
\mathscr{R}_{\alpha}(\Delta)f
by

\displaystyle\mathscr{R}_{\alpha}(\Delta)f:=\mathrm{d}(\alpha-\Delta_{p})^{-\frac{1}{2}}f.

Then we have

\displaystyle\left|{}_{L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)}\left\langle\mathscr{R}_{\alpha}(\Delta)f,\theta\right\rangle_{L^{q}(T^{*}\!M)}\right|\leq 4\int_{M}G_{f}^{\uparrow}(x)G_{\theta}^{\rightarrow}(x)\mathfrak{m}(\mathrm{d}x),\quad\theta\in L^{q}(T^{*}\!M).

(11.1)

Proof.

First we prove the assertion for
p=q=2
. In this case, we have the following identity: For
\theta,\eta\in L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)
,

\displaystyle 4\int_{0}^{\infty}t\,\left\langle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}Q_{t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\theta,\frac{\partial}{\partial t}Q_{t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\eta\right\rangle_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)}\mathrm{d}t=\langle\theta,\eta\rangle_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)}.

(11.2)

Indeed, the left hand side of (
11.2
) is

\displaystyle 4\int_{0}^{\infty}t\left\langle\sqrt{\alpha-{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK}}\right.

\displaystyle Q_{t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\theta,\left.\sqrt{\alpha-{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK}}Q_{t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\eta\right\rangle_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=4\int_{0}^{\infty}t\,\langle(\alpha-{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK})Q_{2t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\theta,\eta\rangle_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\left\langle\int_{0}^{\infty}t\,(\alpha-{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK})Q_{t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\theta\mathrm{d}t,\eta\right\rangle_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)}

\displaystyle=\langle\theta,\eta\rangle_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)},

where we use

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}t\,(\alpha-{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK})Q_{t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\theta\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\alpha s}(\alpha-{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK})P_{s}^{\rm HK}\theta\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}t\left(\frac{t}{2\sqrt{\pi}}e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{4s}}s^{-\frac{3}{2}}\right)\mathrm{d}t\right)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip-8.5359pt\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:Expectation}}}{{=}}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\alpha s}(\alpha-{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK})P_{s}^{\rm HK}\theta\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\partial}{\partial s}\left(e^{-\alpha s}P_{s}^{\rm HK}\theta\right)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=-\left[e^{-\alpha s}P_{s}^{\rm HK}\theta\right]_{0}^{\infty}=\theta.

On the other hand, the intertwining property (
6.3
) yields that

Q_{t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}(\mathrm{d}g)=\mathrm{d}Q_{t}^{(\alpha)}g
holds for
g=(\alpha-\Delta)^{-\frac{1}{2}}f\in D(\mathscr{E})
.
Differentiating the both sides with respect to
t
, we have

\displaystyle(\alpha-{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK})^{\frac{1}{2}}Q_{t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}(\mathrm{d}((\alpha-\Delta)^{-\frac{1}{2}}f))

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}(\alpha-\Delta)^{\frac{1}{2}}Q_{t}^{(\alpha)}(\alpha-\Delta)^{-\frac{1}{2}}f=\mathrm{d}Q_{t}^{(\alpha)}f.

Thus

\displaystyle-\frac{\partial}{\partial t}Q_{t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\left(\mathrm{d}(\alpha-\Delta)^{-\frac{1}{2}}f\right)

\displaystyle=(\alpha-{\Delta\hskip-8.2511pt\Delta}^{\rm\!HK})^{\frac{1}{2}}Q_{t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\left(\mathrm{d}(\alpha-\Delta)^{-\frac{1}{2}}f\right)=\mathrm{d}Q_{t}^{(\alpha)}f.

(11.3)

Set
\eta:=\mathscr{R}_{\alpha}(\Delta)f=\mathrm{d}(\alpha-\Delta)^{-\frac{1}{2}}f
for
f\in L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})
. From
(
11.2
) and (
11.3
),

\displaystyle\left|\langle\mathscr{R}_{\alpha}(\Delta)f,\theta\rangle_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)}\right|

\displaystyle=4\left|\int_{0}^{\infty}t\,\left\langle\mathrm{d}Q_{t}^{(\alpha)}f,\frac{\partial}{\partial t}Q_{t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\theta\right\rangle_{L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)}\mathrm{d}t\right|

\displaystyle\leq 4\int_{0}^{\infty}t\,\int_{M}\left|\mathrm{d}Q_{t}^{(\alpha)}f(x)\right|\cdot\left|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}Q_{t}^{(\alpha),{\rm HK}}\theta(x)\right|\mathfrak{m}(\mathrm{d}x)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq 4\int_{M}G_{f}^{\uparrow}(x)G_{\theta}^{\rightarrow}(x)\mathfrak{m}(\mathrm{d}x).

Then we have (
11.1
) for
f\in L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})
and
\theta\in L^{2}(T^{*}\!M)
with
p=q=2
.

Finally, we prove (
11.1
) for general
f\in L^{p}(M;\mathfrak{m})
and
\theta\in L^{q}(T^{*}\!M)
with
p\in]1,+\infty[
and
q:=p/(p-1)\in]1,+\infty[
. Take
f\in L^{p}(M;\mathfrak{m})
and
\theta\in L^{q}(T^{*}\!M)
. Let
\{f_{n}\}\subset L^{p}(M;\mathfrak{m})\cap L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})

(resp.
\{\theta_{n}\}\subset L^{q}(T^{*}\!M)
) be an approximating sequence to
f\in L^{p}(M;\mathfrak{m})
(resp.
\theta\in L^{q}(T^{*}\!M)
) in

L^{p}(M;\mathfrak{m})
(resp. in
L^{q}(T^{*}\!M)
). Then, for any
g\in L^{p}(M;\mathfrak{m})\cap L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})

\displaystyle\left|{}_{L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)}\left\langle\mathscr{R}_{\alpha}(\Delta)g,\theta_{n}-\theta_{m}\right\rangle_{L^{q}(T^{*}\!M)}\right|

\displaystyle\leq 4\int_{M}G_{g}^{\uparrow}(x)G_{\theta_{n}-\theta_{m}}^{\rightarrow}(x)\mathfrak{m}(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\leq 4\|G_{g}^{\uparrow}\|_{L^{p}(M;\mathfrak{m})}\|G_{\theta_{n}-\theta_{m}}^{\rightarrow}\|_{L^{q}(M;\mathfrak{m})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|g\|_{L^{p}(M;\mathfrak{m})}\|\theta_{n}-\theta_{m}\|_{L^{q}(T^{*}\!M)}\to 0\quad\text{ as }\quad n,m\to\infty.

We set for
\theta\in L^{q}(T^{*}\!M)

\displaystyle{}_{L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)}\left\langle\mathscr{R}_{\alpha}(\Delta)g,\theta\right\rangle_{L^{q}(T^{*}\!M)}:=\lim_{n\to\infty}{}_{L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)}\left\langle\mathscr{R}_{\alpha}(\Delta)g,\theta_{n}\right\rangle_{L^{q}(T^{*}\!M)}.

Then, one can deduce

\displaystyle\left|{}_{L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)}\left\langle\mathscr{R}_{\alpha}(\Delta)g,\theta\right\rangle_{L^{q}(T^{*}\!M)}\right|

\displaystyle\leq 4\int_{M}G_{g}^{\uparrow}(x)G_{\theta}^{\rightarrow}(x)\mathfrak{m}(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\leq 4\|G_{g}^{\uparrow}\|_{L^{p}(M;\mathfrak{m})}\|G_{\theta}^{\rightarrow}\|_{L^{q}(M;\mathfrak{m})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|g\|_{L^{p}(M;\mathfrak{m})}\|\theta\|_{L^{q}(T^{*}\!M)},

where we use
|G_{\theta_{1}}^{\rightarrow}-G_{\theta_{2}}^{\rightarrow}|\leq G_{\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}}^{\rightarrow}
for
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in L^{q}(T^{*}\!M)
.
Similarly, for
\theta\in L^{q}(T^{*}\!M)

\displaystyle\left|{}_{L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)}\left\langle\mathscr{R}_{\alpha}(\Delta)(f_{n}-f_{m}),\theta\right\rangle_{L^{q}(T^{*}\!M)}\right|

\displaystyle\leq 4\int_{M}G_{f_{n}-f_{m}}^{\uparrow}(x)G_{\theta}^{\rightarrow}(x)\mathfrak{m}(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\leq 4\|G_{f_{n}-f_{m}}^{\uparrow}\|_{L^{p}(M;\mathfrak{m})}\|G_{\theta}^{\rightarrow}\|_{L^{q}(M;\mathfrak{m})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f_{n}-f_{m}\|_{L^{p}(M;\mathfrak{m})}\|\theta\|_{L^{q}(T^{*}\!M)}\to 0\quad\text{ as }\quad n,m\to\infty.

Hence, we have (
11.1
) for
f\in L^{p}(M;\mathfrak{m})
and
\theta\in L^{q}(T^{*}\!M)
with
p\in]1,+\infty[
and
q=p/(p-1)\in]1,+\infty[
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1.2
.

By Theorems
10.9
and
10.11
, under the present conditions, we always have

\displaystyle\|G_{\theta}^{\rightarrow}\|_{L^{p}(M;\mathfrak{m})}\lesssim\|\theta\|_{L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)}\quad\text{ for }\quad\theta\in L^{p}(T^{*}\!M),\quad p\in]1,+\infty[.

By Lemma
11.1
, for
f\in L^{p}(M;\mathfrak{m})
and
\theta\in L^{q}(T^{*}\!M)
with
p\in]1,+\infty[
and
q:=p/(p-1)\in]1,+\infty[
,

\displaystyle\left|{}_{L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)}\left\langle\mathscr{R}_{\alpha}(\Delta)f,\theta\right\rangle_{L^{q}(T^{*}\!M)}\right|\lesssim\|G_{f}^{\uparrow}\|_{L^{p}(M;\mathfrak{m})}\|G_{\theta}^{\rightarrow}\|_{L^{q}(M;\mathfrak{m})}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{p}(M;\mathfrak{m})}\|\theta\|_{L^{q}(T^{*}\!M)}.

This implies

\displaystyle\|{R}_{\alpha}(\Delta)f\|_{L^{p}(M;\mathfrak{m})}=\|\mathscr{R}_{\alpha}(\Delta)f\|_{L^{p}(T^{*}\!M)}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{p}(M;\mathfrak{m})}\quad\text{ for }\quad p\in]1,+\infty[\quad\text{ and }\quad\alpha>C_{\kappa}.

∎

12

Examples

It is well-known that (abstract) Wiener space
(B,H,\mu)
satisfies Littlewood-Paley-Stein inequality for
L^{p}(B,K;\mu)
-functions with a real separable Hilbert space
K
(see Shigekawa
[
50
, Chapter 3]
).
Here
L^{p}(B,K;\mu)
is a
K
-valued
L^{p}
-integrable functions.
Though our Theorem
1.2
is not new for
(B,H,\mu)
, we observe the conditions:
Let
(\mathscr{E}^{\rm OU},D(\mathscr{E}^{\rm OU}))
be the Dirichlet form on

L^{2}(B;\mu)
associated to the Ornstein-Uhlenbeck process
{\bf X}^{\rm OU}
and
(T_{t}^{\rm OU})_{t\geq 0}
its associated semigroup on
L^{2}(B;\mu)
.
Let
D_{H}
be the
H
-derivative, that is,
\langle D_{H}F(z),h\rangle_{H}=\lim_{{\varepsilon}\to 0}\frac{F(z+{\varepsilon}h)-F(z)}{{\varepsilon}}
, for a cylindrical function
F
. It is known that

{\sf BE}_{2}(1,\infty)
-condition holds for
(B,\mathscr{E}^{\rm OU},\mu)
(see
[
3
, 13.2]
), that is,

(B,\mathscr{E}^{\rm OU},\mu)
is tamed by
\mu\in S_{K}({\bf X}^{\rm OU})
.
In this case, the measure-valued Ricci lower bound
\kappa=\mu
satisfies
\kappa_{\ll}=\mu
and
\kappa_{\perp}=0
.
It is easy to see that
L^{p}(B,K;\mu)
forms an
L^{p}
-normed
L^{\infty}
-module over
(B,\mu)
. Indeed,
for
f,g\in L^{\infty}(B;\mu)
and
v\in L^{p}(B,K;\mu)
, we see
f\cdot v\in L^{p}(B,K;\mu)

satisfying
(fg)\cdot v=f\cdot(g\cdot v)
and
{\bf 1}_{B}\cdot v=v
, and

|\cdot|_{K}:L^{p}(B,K;\mu)\to L^{p}(B;\mu)
is actually a point-wise norm, i.e.

|f\cdot v|_{K}=|f|\cdot|v|_{K}

\mu
-a.e. and
\|v\|_{L^{p}(B,K;\mu)}=\||v|_{K}\|_{L^{p}(B;\mu)}
hold.
The conclusions in Theorems
10.9
and
10.11
, hence Theorem
1.2
hold for
(B,H,\mu)
and
\alpha>C_{\kappa}=0
in terms of
L^{p}(B,H,\mu)
. Thus
we have the boundedness of
Riesz operator
R_{\alpha}(\Delta^{\rm OU})
on
L^{p}(B;\mu)
.

Moreover, in Kawabi
[
34
]
,
the content of Theorem
1.2
is also announced in the framework of diffusion semigroup of
{\rm P}(\phi)_{1}
-quantum fields in infinite volume.
More concretely, let
E
be a Hilbert space defined by
E:=L^{2}(\mathbb{R},\mathbb{R}^{d};e^{-2\lambda\chi(x)}\mathrm{d}x)
with a fixed
\chi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
satisfying
\chi(x)=|x|
for
|x|\geq 1
and another Hilbert space
H:=L^{2}(\mathbb{R},\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathrm{d}x)
.
They consider a Dirichlet form
(\mathscr{E},D(\mathscr{E}))
on
L^{2}(E;\mu)
associated with the diffusion process
{\bf X}
on an infinite volume
path space
C(\mathbb{R},\mathbb{R}^{d})
with (
U
-)Gibbs measures
\mu
associated with the (formal) Hamiltonian

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}(w):=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|w^{\prime}(x)|^{2}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\mathbb{R}}U(w(x))\mathrm{d}x,

where
U:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
is an interactions potential satisfying
{\nabla^{2}}U\geq-K_{1}

with some
K_{1}\in\mathbb{R}

(see
[
35
, 4.1]
).
Then the
L^{2}
-semigroup
(P_{t})_{t\geq 0}
associated to
(\mathscr{E},D(\mathscr{E}))
satisfies the following gradient estimate

\displaystyle|D(P_{t}f)(w)|_{H}\leq e^{K_{1}t}P_{t}(|Df|_{H})(w)\quad\text{ for }\quad\mu\text{-a.e.~}w\in E,

which is equivalent to
{\sf BE}_{2}(-K_{1},\infty)
-condition.
Here
Df
is a closed extension of the Fréchet derivative
Df:E\to H
for cylindrical function
f
.
Hence
(E,\mathscr{E},\mu)
is tamed by
\kappa:=-K_{1}\mu
with
K_{1}\mu\in S_{K}({\bf X})
.
In this case, the measure-valued Ricci lower bound
\kappa
satisfies
\kappa_{\ll}=-K_{1}\mu
and

\kappa_{\perp}=0
. Denote by
L^{p}(E,H;\mu)
an
H
-valued
L^{p}
-integrable functions.

L^{p}(E,H;\mu)
is an
L^{p}
-normed
L^{\infty}
-module as remarked above.
Then, Theorem
1.2
in terms of
L^{p}(E,H;\mu)

holds for
(E,\mathscr{E},\mu)
under any
\alpha>C_{\kappa}=K_{1}\lor 0
and
p\in]1,+\infty[
.

Denote by
(M,g,\mathfrak{m})
, a complete weighted Riemannian manifold without boundary, where
\mathfrak{m}:=e^{-\phi}\mathfrak{v}
is the
weighted measure with a potential function
\phi\in C^{2}(M)
and volume measure
\mathfrak{v}
. Let
(\mathscr{E},D(\mathscr{E}))
be the Dirichlet form on
L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})
defined by
the closure of
(\mathscr{E},C_{c}^{\infty}(M))
:

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}(f,g)=\int_{M}\langle\nabla f,\nabla g\rangle\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{m},\quad f,g\in C_{c}^{\infty}(M).

The
L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})
-generator
L
associated with
(\mathscr{E},D(\mathscr{E}))
is given by

Lf=\Delta f-\langle\nabla f,\nabla\phi\rangle
for
f\in C_{c}^{\infty}(M)
.
Denote by
{\bf X}
the
\mathfrak{m}
-symmetric diffusion process associated with
(\mathscr{E},D(\mathscr{E}))
.
Let
{\rm Ric}_{\phi}^{\infty}
be Bakry-Émery Ricci curvature defined by

\displaystyle{\rm Ric}_{\phi}^{\infty}(u,v):={\rm Ric}_{g}(u,v)+{\rm Hess}\,\phi(u,v),\quad u,v\in TM.

We assume
{\rm Ric}_{\phi}^{\infty}\geq kg
for a measurable function
k
on
M
. In this case, measure-valued Ricci lower bound
\kappa
satisfies

\kappa_{\ll}=k\mathfrak{m}
and
\kappa_{\perp}=0
. Theorem
10.9
in the present setting is proved by X.-D. Li
[
40
, Theorem 4.3]
. Based on this, X.-D. Li
[
40
, Theorem 2.1]
obtained the boundedness of Riesz transform
\mathscr{R}_{\alpha}(L)
on
L^{p}(M;\mathfrak{m})
for
p\in[2,+\infty[
under
k\in S_{K}({\bf X})
(or under a weaker assumption). Theorem
10.11
in the present setting is proved by Bakry
[
9
]
.
Thus we have the boundedness of Riesz transform
\mathscr{R}_{\alpha}(L)
on
L^{p}(M;\mathfrak{m})
for
p\in]1,2[
and
\alpha>C_{\kappa}:=(-\inf_{M}k)\lor 0
under
the lower boundedness of
k
.

In the rest, we expose new examples.

Example 12.1

(RCD spaces)
.

A metric measure space
(M,{\sf d},\mathfrak{m})
is a complete separable metric space
(M,{\sf d})
with a
\sigma
-finite Borel measure with
\mathfrak{m}(B)<\infty
for any bounded Borel set
B
. We assume
\mathfrak{m}
has full topological support, i.e.,
\mathfrak{m}(G)>0
for non-empty open set
G
.

Any metric open ball is denoted by
B_{r}(x):=\{y\in M\mid{\sf d}(x,y)<r\}
for
r>0
and
x\in M
.
A subset
B
of
M
is said to be bounded if it is included in a metric open ball.
Denote by
C([0,1],M)
the space of continuous curve defined on the unit interval
[0,1]
equipped the distance
{\sf d}_{\infty}(\gamma,\eta):=\sup_{t\in[0,1]}{\sf d}(\gamma_{t},\eta_{t})
for every
\gamma,\eta\in C([0,1],M)
. This turn
C([0,1],M)
into complete separable metric space.
Next we consider the set of
2
-absolutely continuous curves, denoted by
AC^{q}([0,1],M)
, is the subset of
\gamma\in C([0,1],M)
so that there exists
g\in L^{q}(0,1)
satisfying

\displaystyle{\sf d}(\gamma_{t},\gamma_{s})\leq\int_{s}^{t}g(r)\mathrm{d}r,\quad s<t\text{ in }[0,1].

Recall that for any
\gamma\in AC^{2}([0,1],M)
, there exists a minimal a.e. function
g\in L^{2}(0,1)
satisfying the above, called
metric speed
denoted by
|\dot{\gamma}_{t}|
, which is defined as

|\dot{\gamma}_{t}|:=\lim_{h\downarrow 0}{\sf d}(\gamma_{t+h},\gamma_{t})/h
for
\gamma\in AC^{2}([0,1],M)
,

|\dot{\gamma}_{t}|:=+\infty
otherwise.
We define the kinetic energy functional
C([0,1],M)\ni\gamma\mapsto{\sf Ke}_{t}(\gamma):=\int_{0}^{1}|\dot{\gamma}_{t}|^{2}\mathrm{d}t
, if
\gamma\in AC^{2}([0,1],M)
,
{\sf Ke}_{t}(\gamma):=+\infty
otherwise.

Definition 12.2

(
2
-test plan)
.

Let
(M,{\sf d},\mathfrak{m})
be a metric measure space.
A measure
\pi
\in\mathscr{P}(C([0,1],M))
is said to be a
2
-test plan, provided

(i)

there exists
C>0
so that
({\sf e}_{t})_{\sharp}
\pi
\leq C\mathfrak{m}
for every
t\in[0,1]
;

(ii)

we have
\int_{C([0,1],M)}{\sf Ke}_{2}(\gamma)
\pi
(\mathrm{d}\gamma)<\infty
.

Definition 12.3

(Sobolev space
W^{\hskip 0.85355pt1,2}(M)
)
.

A Borel function
f\in L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})
belongs to
W^{1,2}(M)
,
provided there exists a
G\in L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})
,
called
2
-weak upper gradient
of
f
so that

\displaystyle\int_{C([0,1],M)}|f(\gamma_{1})-f(\gamma_{0})|\text{\boldmath$\pi$}(\mathrm{d}\gamma)\leq\int_{C([0,1],M)}\int_{0}^{1}G(\gamma_{t})|\dot{\gamma}_{t}|\mathrm{d}t\text{\boldmath$\pi$}(\mathrm{d}\gamma),\quad\text{$\forall${\boldmath$\pi$}
$2$-test plan}.

(12.1)

The assignment
(t,\gamma)\mapsto G(\gamma_{t})|\dot{\gamma}_{t}|
is Borel measurable (see
[
30
, Remark 2.1.9]
) and the right hand side of
(
12.1
) is finite for
G\in L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})_{+}
(see
[
29
, (2.5)]
). These shows not only the finiteness of the right hand side of (
12.1
) but also the continuity of the assignment

L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})\ni G\mapsto\int_{C([0,1],M)}\int_{0}^{1}G(\gamma_{t})|\dot{\gamma}_{t}|\mathrm{d}t\text{\boldmath$\pi$}(\mathrm{d}\gamma)
. This, combined with the closedness of the convex combination of the
2
-weak upper gradient, shows that the set of
2
-weak upper gradient of a given Borel function
f
is a closed convex subset of
L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})
. The minimal
p
-weak upper gradient, denoted by
|Df|_{2}
is then the element of
minimal
L^{p}
-norm in this class. Also, by making use of the lattice property of the set of
2
-weak
upper gradient, such a minimality is also in the
\mathfrak{m}
-a.e. sense (see
[
16
, Proposition 2.17 and Theorem 2.18]
).

Then,
W^{1,2}(M)
forms a Banach space equipped with the following norm:
equipped with the norm

\displaystyle\|f\|_{W^{1,2}(M)}:=\left(\|f\|^{2}_{L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})}+\||Df|_{2}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},\quad f\in W^{1,2}(M).

It is in general false
that
(W^{1,2}(M),\|\cdot\|_{W^{1,2}(M)})
is a Hilbert space.
When this occurs, we say that
(M,{\sf d},\mathfrak{m})
is
infinitesimally Hilbertian
(see

[
26
]
). Equivalently, we call
(X,{\sf d},\mathfrak{m})
infinitesimally Hilbertian
provided the following
parallelogram identity
holds:

\displaystyle 2|Df|_{2}^{2}+2|Dg|_{2}^{2}=|D(f+g)|_{2}^{2}+|D(f-g)|_{2}^{2},\quad\mathfrak{m}\text{-a.e.}\quad\forall f,g\in W^{1,2}(M).

(12.2)

For simplicity, when
p=2
, we omit the suffix
2
from
|Df|_{2}
for
f\in W^{1,2}(M)
, i.e. we write
|Df|
instead of
|Df|_{2}
for
f\in W^{1,2}(M)
.
Under (
12.2
), we can give a bilinear form
\langle D\cdot,D\cdot\rangle:W^{1,2}(M)\times W^{1,2}(M)\to L^{1}(M;\mathfrak{m})
which is defined by

\displaystyle\langle Df,Dg\rangle:=\frac{1}{4}|D(f+g)|^{2}-\frac{1}{4}|D(f-g)|^{2},\quad f,g\in W^{1,2}(M).

Moreover, under the infinitesimally Hilbertian condition,
the bilinear form
(\mathscr{E},D(\mathscr{E}))
defined by

\displaystyle D(\mathscr{E}):=W^{1,2}(M),\quad\mathscr{E}(f,g):=\frac{1}{2}\int_{M}\langle Df,Dg\rangle\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{m}

is a strongly local Dirichlet form on
L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})
.
Denote by
(P_{t})_{t\geq 0}
the
\mathfrak{m}
-symmetric semigroup on
L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})
associated with
(\mathscr{E},D(\mathscr{E}))
.
Under (
12.2
), let
(\Delta,D(\Delta))
be the

L^{2}
-generator associated with
(\mathscr{E},D(\mathscr{E}))
similarly defined as in (
1.1
) before.

Definition 12.4

(RCD-spaces)
.

A metric measure space
(X,{\sf d},\mathfrak{m})
is said to be an
RCD
(K,\infty)
-space
if
it satisfies
the following conditions:

(1)

(M,{\sf d},\mathfrak{m})
is infinitesimally Hilbertian.

(2)

There exist
x_{0}\in M
and constants
c,C>0
such that

\mathfrak{m}(B_{r}(x_{0}))\leq C\mathrm{e}^{cr^{2}}
.

(3)

If
f\in W^{1,2}(M)
satisfies

|Df|\leq 1

\mathfrak{m}
-a.e., then
f
has a
1
-Lipschitz representative.

(4)

For any
f\in D(\Delta)

with
\Delta f\in W^{1,2}(M)

and
g\in D(\Delta)\cap L^{\infty}(M;\mathfrak{m})

with
g\geq 0
and
\Delta g\in L^{\infty}(M;\mathfrak{m})
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{M}|Df|^{2}\Delta g\,\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{m}-\int_{M}\langle Df,D\Delta f\rangle g\,\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{m}\geq K\int_{M}|Df|^{2}g\,\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{m}

Let
N\in[1,+\infty[
.
A metric measure space
(M,{\sf d},\mathfrak{m})
is said to be an
RCD
{}^{*}(K,N)
-space
if
it is an
RCD
(K,\infty)
-space and
for any
f\in D(\Delta)

with
\Delta f\in W^{1,2}(M)

and
g\in D(\Delta)\cap L^{\infty}(M;\mathfrak{m})

with
g\geq 0
and
\Delta g\in L^{\infty}(M;\mathfrak{m})
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{M}|Df|^{2}\Delta g\,\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{m}-\int_{M}\langle Df,D\Delta f\rangle g\,\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{m}\geq K\int_{M}|Df|^{2}g\,\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{m}+\frac{1}{N}\int_{M}(\Delta f)^{2}g\,\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{m}.

Remark 12.5
.

(1)

It is shown in
[
13
,
41
]
that for

N\in[1,+\infty[

RCD
(K,N)
-space is equivalent to

RCD
{}^{*}(K,N)
-space, where
RCD
{}^{*}(K,N)
-space (resp.
RCD
{}^{*}(K,N)
-space) is defined to be a
CD
{}^{*}(K,N)
-space
(resp.
CD
{}^{*}(K,N)
-space) having infinitesimal Hilbertian condition.
Originally, for
N\in[1,+\infty]
, the notion of
CD
(K,N)
-spaces was defined by Lott-Villani
[
42
]
and Sturm
[
52
,
53
]
. Later, Bacher-Sturm
[
8
]
gave the notion of

CD
{}^{*}(K,N)
-space as a variant for
N\in[1,+\infty[
. Finally, Erbar-Kuwada-Sturm
[
21
]
invented the notion of

CD
{}^{e}(K,N)
-space, so-called the metric measure space satisfying entropic curvature dimension condition under
N\in[1,+\infty[
and prove that
RCD
{}^{*}(K,N)
-space coincides with
RCD
{}^{e}(K,N)
-space, i.e.,

CD
{}^{e}(K,N)
-space satisfying infinitesimal Hilbertian condition.
The notion
RCD
(K,\infty)
-space, i.e.,
CD
(K,\infty)
-space satisfying
infinitesimal Hilbertian condition, was firstly considered in
[
5
,
7
]
, and
the
RCD
(K,N)
-space under
N\in[1,+\infty[
was also given by
[
21
,
26
]
.
Moreover, under
N\in[1,+\infty[
,
RCD
(K,N)
-space (or
RCD
{}^{*}(K,N)
-space) is a locally compact separable metric space, consequently,
\mathfrak{m}
becomes a Radon measure.

(2)

If
(M,{\sf d},\mathfrak{m})
is an
RCD
(K,N)
-space under
N\in[1,+\infty[
,
it enjoys the Bishop-Gromov inequality:
Let
\kappa:=K/(N-1)
if
N>1
and
\kappa:=0
if
N=1
. We set
\omega_{N}:=\frac{\pi^{N/2}}{\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{N/2}e^{-t}\mathrm{d}t}
(volume of unit bal in
\mathbb{R}^{N}
provided
N\in\mathbb{N}
) and

V_{\kappa}(r):=\omega_{N}\int_{0}^{r}\mathfrak{s}_{\kappa}^{N-1}(t)\mathrm{d}t
.
Then

\displaystyle\frac{\mathfrak{m}(B_{R}(x))}{V_{\kappa}(R)}\leq\frac{\mathfrak{m}(B_{r}(x))}{V_{\kappa}(r)},\qquad x\in M,\qquad 0<r<R.

(12.3)

Here

\mathfrak{s}_{\kappa}(s)
is the solution to Jacobi equation
\mathfrak{s}_{\kappa}^{\prime\prime}(s)+\kappa\mathfrak{s}_{\kappa}(s)=0
with

\mathfrak{s}_{\kappa}(0)=0
,
\mathfrak{s}_{\kappa}^{\prime}(0)=1
. More concretely,
\mathfrak{s}_{\kappa}(s)
is given by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{s}_{\kappa}(s):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{\sin\sqrt{\kappa}s}{\sqrt{\kappa}}&\kappa>0,\\
s&\kappa=0,\\
\frac{\sinh\sqrt{-\kappa}s}{\sqrt{-\kappa}}&\kappa<0.\end{array}\right.

(3)

If
(M,{\sf d},\mathfrak{m})
is an
\mathsf{RCD}(K,\infty)
-space, it satisfies the following Bakry-Émery estimate:

\displaystyle|DP_{t}f|\leq e^{-Kt}P_{t}|Df|\quad\mathfrak{m}\text{-a.e.}\quad for\quad f\in W^{1,2}(M),

(12.4)

in particular,
P_{t}f\in W^{1,2}(M)

(see
[
46
, Corollary 3.5]
,
[
28
, Proposition 3.1]
).

(4)

If
(M,{\sf d},\mathfrak{m})
is an
\mathsf{RCD}(K,N)
-space with
N\in[1,+\infty[
, then,
for any
f\in{\rm Lip}(M)

{\rm lip}(f)=|Df|

\mathfrak{m}
-a.e. holds,
because
\mathsf{RCD}(K,N)
with
N\in[1,+\infty[
admits the local volume doubling and a weak local
(1,2)
-Poincaré inequality (see
[
16
, §5 and §6]
).

By definition, for
{\sf RCD}(K,N)
-space
(M,{\sf d},\mathfrak{m})
,
(M,\mathscr{E},\mathfrak{m})

satisfies
{\sf BE}_{2}(K,N)
-condition and
K^{\pm}\mathfrak{m}
is always a Kato class smooth measure,
hence it is a tamed Dirichlet space by
K\mathfrak{m}
.
In this case, the measure-valued Ricci lower bound
\kappa=K\mathfrak{m}
satisfies
\kappa_{\ll}=K\mathfrak{m}
and
\kappa_{\perp}=0
.
So Theorem
1.2
holds for
(M,{\sf d},\mathfrak{m})
and
\alpha>C_{\kappa}=(-K)\lor 0
.

Example 12.6

(Riemannian manifolds with boundary)
.

Let
(M,g)
be a smooth Riemannian manifold with boundary
\partial M
.
Denote by
\mathfrak{v}:={\rm vol}_{g}
the Riemannian volume measure induced by
g
, and by

\mathfrak{s}
the surface measure on
\partial M

(see
[
12
, §1.2]
).
If
\partial M\neq\emptyset
, then
\partial M
is a smooth co-dimension
1
submanifold of
M
and it becomes Riemannian
when endowed with the pulback metric

\displaystyle\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{j}:=j^{*}\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle,\qquad\langle u,v:=g(u,v)\quad\text{ for }\quad u,v\in TM.

under the natural inclusion
j:\partial M\to M
. The map
j
induces a natural inclusion
d_{j}:T\partial M\to TM|_{\partial M}
which is not surjective. In particular, the vector bundles
T\partial M
and
TM|_{\partial M}
do not coincide.

Let
\mathfrak{m}
be a Borel measure on
M
which is locally equivalent to
\mathfrak{v}
.
Let
D(\mathscr{E}):=W^{1,2}(M^{\circ})
be the Sobolev space with respect to

\mathfrak{m}
defined in the usual sense on
M^{\circ}:=M\setminus\partial M
. Define
\mathscr{E}:W^{1,2}(M^{\circ})\to[0,+\infty[

by

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}(f):=\int_{M^{\circ}}|\nabla f|^{2}\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{m}

and the quantity
\mathscr{E}(f,g)
,
f,g\in W^{1,2}(M^{\circ})
, by polarization. Then
(\mathscr{E},D(\mathscr{E}))
becomes a strongly local regular Dirichlet form on
L^{2}(M;\mathfrak{m})
, since
C_{c}^{\infty}(M)
is a dense set of
D(\mathscr{E})
.
Let
k:M^{\circ}\to\mathbb{R}
and
\ell:\partial M\to\mathbb{R}
be continuous functions providing lower bounds on the Ricci curvature and the second fundamental form of
\partial M
, respectively.
Suppose that
M
is compact and
\mathfrak{m}=\mathfrak{v}
. Then
(M,\mathscr{E},\mathfrak{m})
is tamed by

\displaystyle\kappa:=k\mathfrak{v}+\ell\mathfrak{s},

because
\mathfrak{v},\mathfrak{s}\in S_{K}({\bf X})
(see
[
22
, Theorem 2.36]
and
[
33
, Theorem 5.1]
). Then one can apply Theorem
1.2
to
(M,\mathscr{E},\mathfrak{m})
.
Remark that Theorems
10.9
and
10.11

are proved by
Shigekawa
[
49
, Propositions 6.2 and 6.4]
in the framework of compact smooth Riemannian manifold with boundary.

More generally, if
M
is regularly exhaustible, i.e., there exists an increasing sequence
(M_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
of domains
M_{n}\subset M^{\circ}
with smooth boundary
\partial M_{n}
such that
g
is smooth on
M_{n}
and the following properties hold:

(1)

The closed sets
(\overline{M}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
constitute an
\mathscr{E}
-nest for
(\mathscr{E},W^{1,2}(M))
.

(2)

For all compact sets
K\subset M^{\circ}
there exists
N\in\mathbb{N}
such tht
K\subset M_{n}
for all
n\geq N
.

(3)

There are lower bounds
\ell_{n}:\partial M_{n}\to\mathbb{R}
for the curvature of
\partial M_{n}
with
\ell_{n}=\ell
on
\partial M\cap\partial M_{n}
such that the distributions
\kappa_{n}=k\mathfrak{v}_{n}+\ell_{n}\mathfrak{s}_{n}
are uniformly
2
-moderate in
the sense that

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\sup_{x\in M_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{(n)}\left[e^{-2A_{t}^{\kappa_{n}}}\right]<\infty,

(12.5)

where
\mathfrak{v}_{n}
is the volume measure of
M_{n}
and
\mathfrak{s}_{n}
is the surface measure of
\partial M_{n}
.

Suppose
\mathfrak{m}=\mathfrak{v}
.
Then
(M,\mathscr{E},\mathfrak{m})
is tamed by
\kappa=k\mathfrak{v}+\ell\mathfrak{s}

(see
[
22
, Theorem 4.5]
). In this case,
\kappa_{\ll}=k\mathfrak{v}
and
\kappa_{\perp}=\ell\mathfrak{s}
, hence Theorem
1.2
holds for
(M,\mathscr{E},\mathfrak{m})
provided

\kappa^{+}\in S_{D}({\bf X})
,
\kappa^{-}\in S_{K}({\bf X})
for
p\in[2,+\infty[
,
or
k
is bounded below and
\ell\geq 0
for
p\in]1,2[
.

Let
Y
be the domain defined by

\displaystyle Y:=\{(x,y,z)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\mid z>\phi(\sqrt{x^{2}+y^{2}})\},

where
\phi:[0,+\infty[\to[0,+\infty[
is
C^{2}
on
]0,+\infty[
with
\phi(r):=r-r^{2-\alpha}
,
\alpha\in]0,1[
for

r\in[0,1]
,
\phi
is a constant for
r\geq 2
and
\phi^{\prime\prime}(r)\leq 0
for
r\in[0,+\infty[
.
Let
\mathfrak{m}_{Y}
be the
3
-dimensional Lebesgue measure restricted to
Y
and

\sigma_{\partial Y}
the
2
-dimensional Hausdorff measure on
\partial Y
. Denote by

\mathscr{E}_{Y}
the Dirichlet form on
L^{2}(Y;\mathfrak{m})
with Neumann boundary conditions.
The smallest eigenvalue of the second fundamental form of
\partial Y
can be given by

\displaystyle\ell(r,\phi)=\frac{\phi^{\prime\prime}(r)}{(1+|\phi^{\prime}(r)|^{2})^{3/2}}(\leq 0),

for
r\leq 1
and
\ell=0
for
r\geq 2
. It is proved in
[
22
, Theorem 4.6]
that
the Dirichlet space
(Y,\mathscr{E}_{Y},\mathfrak{m}_{Y})
is tamed by

\displaystyle\kappa=\ell\sigma_{\partial Y}.

In this case, the measure-valued Ricci lower bound
\kappa
satisfies

|\kappa|=|\ell|\sigma_{\partial Y}\in S_{K}({\bf X})
(see
[
22
, Lemma 2.34, Theorem 2.36, Proof of Theorem 4.6]
),
\kappa_{\ll}=0
and
\kappa_{\perp}=\ell\sigma_{\partial Y}
. Then
Theorem
1.2
holds for
(Y,\mathscr{E}_{Y},\mathfrak{m}_{Y})
and
\alpha>C_{\kappa}
if
p\in[2,+\infty[
, or
p\in]1,2]
under
\ell\geq 0
.

Example 12.7

(Configuration space over metric measure spaces)
.

Let
(M,g)
be a complete smooth Riemannian manifold without boundary.
The configuration space
\Upsilon
over
M
is the space of all locally finite point measures, that is,

\displaystyle\Upsilon:=\{\gamma\in\mathcal{M}(M)\mid\gamma(K)\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}\quad\text{ for all compact sets}\quad K\subset M\}.

In the seminal paper Albeverio-Kondrachev-Röckner
[
1
]
identified a natural geometry on
\Upsilon
by lifting the geometry of
M
to
\Upsilon
. In particular, there exists a natural gradient
\nabla^{\Upsilon}
, divergence
{\rm div}^{\Upsilon}
and
Laplace operator
\Delta^{\Upsilon}
on
\Upsilon
. It is shown in
[
1
]
that the Poisson point measure
\pi
on

\Upsilon
is the unique (up to intensity) measure on
\Upsilon
under which the gradient and divergence become
dual operator in
L^{2}(\Upsilon;\pi)
. Hence, the Poisson measure
\pi
is the natural volume measure on
\Upsilon

and
\Upsilon
can be seen as an infinite dimensional Riemannian manifold. The canonical Dirichlet form

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}(F)=\int_{\Upsilon}|\nabla^{\Upsilon}F|_{\gamma}^{2}\pi(\mathrm{d}\gamma)

constructed in
[
1
, Theorem 6.1]
is quasi-regular and strongly local
and it induces the heat semigroup
T_{t}^{\Upsilon}
and a Brownian motion
{\bf X}^{\Upsilon}
on
\Upsilon
which can be identified with the independent infinite particle process. If
{\rm Ric}_{g}\geq K
on
M
with
K\in\mathbb{R}
, then
(\Upsilon,\mathscr{E}^{\Upsilon},\pi)
is tamed by

\kappa:=K\pi
with
|\kappa|\in S_{K}({\bf X}^{\Upsilon})
(see
[
21
, Theorem 4.7]
and
[
22
, Theorem 3.6]
).
Then one can apply Theorem
1.2
for
(\Upsilon,\mathscr{E}^{\Upsilon},\pi)
.

More generally, in Dello Schiavo-Suzuki
[
18
]
, configuration space
\Upsilon
over proper complete and separable metric space
(M,{\sf d})
is considered. The configuration space
\Upsilon
is endowed with the
vage topology

\tau_{V}
, induced by duality with continuous compactly supported functions on
M
, and with a reference Borel probability measure
\mu
satisfying
[
18
, Assumption 2.17]
, commonly understood as the law of a proper point process on
M
. In
[
18
]
,
they constructed the strongly local Dirichlet form
\mathscr{E}^{\Upsilon}
defined to be the
L^{2}(\Upsilon;\mu)
-closure of a certain pre-Dirichlet form on a class of certain cylinder functions and prove its quasi-regularity for a wide class of measures
\mu
and base spaces (see
[
18
, Proposition 3.9 and Theorem 3.45]
). Moreover, in
Dello Schiavo-Suzuki
[
19
]
, for any fixed
K\in\mathbb{R}
they prove that a Dirichlet form
(\mathscr{E},D(\mathscr{E}))
with its
carré-du-champ
\Gamma
satisfies
{\sf BE}_{2}(K,\infty)
if and only if the Dirichlet form
(\mathscr{E}^{\Upsilon},D(\mathscr{E}^{\Upsilon}))
on
L^{2}(\Upsilon;\mu)
with its carré-du-champ
\Gamma^{\Upsilon}
satisfies
{\sf BE}_{2}(K,\infty)
.
Hence, if
(M,\mathscr{E},\mathfrak{m})
is tamed by
K\mathfrak{m}
with
|K|\mathfrak{m}\in S_{K}({\bf X})
, then
(\Upsilon,\mathscr{E}^{\Upsilon},\mu)
is tamed by
\kappa:=K\mu
with
|\kappa|\in S_{K}({\bf X}^{\Upsilon})
. In this case, the measure-valued Ricci lower bound
\kappa
satisfies
\kappa_{\ll}=K\mu
and
\kappa_{\perp}=0
.
Then Theorem
1.2
holds for
(\Upsilon,\mathscr{E}^{\Upsilon},\mu)
and any
p\in]1,+\infty[

under the suitable class of measures
\mu
defined in
[
18
, Assumption 2.17]
.

(K,\infty)
-spaces during the preparation of this paper.
